Appendix A
Additional Notations

used throughout the appendix include
\left\lceil x\right\rceil
,
a\vee b
,
|S|
,
|x|
,
\circ
,
\mu\ll\nu
,
T_{\#}\mu
,
\|\mu\|_{TV}
,
\mathcal{O}(a_{n})
,
\operatorname{Im}(f)
,
T=\left(V,E_{T}\right)
,
\partial_{T}\left(B\right)
,
\mathrm{TV}_{T}\left(\mathbf{a}\right)
,
\partial f(u)
,
\partial_{u}\ell(u,y)
,
\mathrm{Rad}
,
\widehat{\mathfrak{R}}_{n}(\mathcal{F})
,
\mathfrak{R}_{n}(\mathcal{F})
,
\mathrm{diam}(\mathcal{X})
,
\operatorname{sign}(u)
,
\supp(f)
,
C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
.

We denote by
\left\lceil x\right\rceil
the ceiling of
x\in\mathbb{R}
. For
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
, we write
a\vee b\coloneqq\max\left\{a,b\right\}
. For a finite set
S
,
|S|
denotes its cardinality. For
x\in\mathbb{R}
,
|x|
denotes its absolute value. The composition of function
f
and
g
is denoted by
f\circ g
. Let
\mu,\nu\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{U})
;
\mu
is said to be absolutely continuous with respect to
\nu
(denoted by
\mu\ll\nu
) if for every measurable set
A
on
U
,
\nu(A)=0
implies
\mu(A)=0
. Let
(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{X}})
and
(\mathcal{Y},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{Y}})
be measurable spaces, and let
\mu
be a probability measure on
(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{X}})
. For a measurable map
T:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{Y}
, the pushforward measure of
\mu
under
T
, denoted by
T_{\#}\mu
, is the measure on
(\mathcal{Y},\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{Y}})
defined by

\displaystyle(T_{\#}\mu)(B):=\mu\left(T^{-1}(B)\right),\qquad\forall B\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{Y}}.

It is known that for every function
f\in L^{1}(\mathcal{Y},T_{\#}\mu)
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{Y}}f(y)\mathrm{d}(T_{\#}\mu)(y)=\int_{\mathcal{X}}f(T(x))\mathrm{d}\mu(x).

Let
\mu
be a finite signed measure on a measurable space
(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{A})
. The total variation norm of
\mu
is defined as

\displaystyle\|\mu\|_{TV}

\displaystyle:=\sup\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}|\mu(A_{i})|:(A_{i})_{i=1}^{\infty}\text{ is a partition of }\mathcal{H};A_{i}\in\mathcal{A}\text{ for }i\in\mathbb{Z}^{>0}\right\}.

Finite signed measures on
(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{A})
form a vector space, which is a Banach space when enriched with the total variation norm. For positive sequences
(a_{n})_{n=1}^{\infty}
and
(b_{n})_{n=1}^{\infty}
,
b_{n}=\mathcal{O}(a_{n})
if there exist
C>0
and
n_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}^{>0}
such that for all
n\geq n_{0}
,
a_{n}\leq Cb_{n}
holds. Let
\mathcal{X}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
be a compact set. A subset
A\subseteq\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X})
is called relatively compact in
(\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X}),\|\cdot\|_{\infty})
if its closure
\overline{A}
is compact in the metric
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{\infty}
; equivalently, every sequence in
A
, has a subsequence convergent in
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X})
. For a function
f:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{Y}
, its image is denoted by
\operatorname{Im}(f):=\{f(x)\,:\,x\in\mathcal{X}\}
. Let
n\geq 2
, a tree
T=\left(V,E_{T}\right)
on
[n]
is a connected, acyclic undirected graph with vertex set
V=[n]
and edge set
E_{T}\subseteq\left\{\left\{i,j\right\}:i,j\in[n],\,i\neq j\right\}
. For a subset
B\subseteq[n]
, its tree boundary size is defined by

\displaystyle\partial_{T}\left(B\right):=\sum_{\left\{i,j\right\}\in E_{T}}\left|\mathds{1}_{B}\left(i\right)-\mathds{1}_{B}\left(j\right)\right|,

in other words,
\partial_{T}(B)
counts the number of edges of the tree
T
having one endpoint in
B
and the other in its complement
[n]\setminus B
. Let
n\geq 2
, for a tree
T=\left(V,E_{T}\right)
on
[n]
and
\mathbf{a}=\left(a_{1},\dots,a_{n}\right)\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, define its tree total variation by

\displaystyle\mathrm{TV}_{T}\left(\mathbf{a}\right)\coloneqq\sum_{\left\{i,j\right\}\in E_{T}}\left|a_{i}-a_{j}\right|.

Let
f:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
be a convex function. The subdifferential of
f
at a point
u\in\mathbb{R}
, denoted by
\partial f(u)
, is the set of all real numbers
g
satisfying
f(v)\geq f(u)+g\,(v-u)
for all
v\in\mathbb{R}
. For a convex function
f:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
, the subdifferential is nonempty
at every point
u\in\mathbb{R}
. Each
g\in\partial f(u)
is called a subgradient of
f
at
u
.
The notation
\partial_{u}\ell(u,y)
refers to the subdifferential of
\ell(\cdot,y)
at point
u
while keeping
y\in\mathcal{Y}
fixed.
Let
\mathrm{Rad}
denote the Rademacher distribution on
\{-1,+1\}
. A vector
\bm{\sigma}=(\sigma_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
drawn from
\mathrm{Rad}^{n}
consists of
n
independent Rademacher variables, where
\Pr(\sigma_i = 1)=\Pr(\sigma_i = -1)=\tfrac{1}{2}
. Let
\mathcal{F}
be a class of measurable real-valued functions on
\mathcal{X}
, and
let
\mathcal{D}_{n}=\{\mathbf{x}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}\subset\mathcal{X}
be a fixed dataset.
The empirical Rademacher complexity of
\mathcal{F}
is

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathfrak{R}}_{n}(\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{Rad}^{n}}\left[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{i}f(\mathbf{x}_{i})\right],

(34)

and its expectation with respect to

(X_{i})_{i=1}^{n}\sim\nu_{X}^{n}
is

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}_{n}(\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{X}^{n}}\left[\widehat{\mathfrak{R}}_{n}(\mathcal{F})\right]=\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{Rad}^{n},\nu_{X}^{n}}\left[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{i}f(X_{i})\right],

(35)

is called the Rademacher complexity of
\mathcal{F}
. Let
\mathcal{X}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
, define
\mathrm{diam}(\mathcal{X}):=\sup_{x,y\in\mathcal{X}}\|x-y\|_{2}
;
\mathrm{diam}(\mathcal{X})<\infty
if
\mathcal{X}
is compact. We denote by
\operatorname{sign}:\mathbb{R}\to[-1,1]
the set-valued function defined as

\displaystyle\operatorname{sign}(u):=\begin{cases}\{+1\},&\text{ if }u>0,\\[3.0pt]
[-1,1],&\text{ if }u=0,\\[3.0pt]
\{-1\},&\text{ if }u<0.\end{cases}

Let
\mathcal{X}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
be a compact set and let
\mathcal{F}\subseteq\{f:\mathcal{X}\to\mathbb{R}\}
be a family of real-valued functions. If for every
\varepsilon>0
there exists
\delta>0
such that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y}\|_{2}<\delta\ \Rightarrow\ |f(\mathbf{x})-f(\mathbf{y})|<\varepsilon\quad\text{for all }f\in\mathcal{F}\text{ and all }\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}\in\mathcal{X},

then
\mathcal{F}
is called equicontinuous on
\mathcal{X}
. A functional
J:\mathcal{X}\to[-\infty,+\infty]
is lower semicontinuous (l.s.c.) if
for every sequence
(\mathbf{x}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\mathcal{X}
converging to
\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X}
,

\displaystyle J(\mathbf{x})\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}J(\mathbf{x}_{n}).

The support of a function
f:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
is defined as

\displaystyle\supp(f):=\overline{\{x\in\mathbb{R}:f(x)\neq 0\}}.

We denote by
C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
the space of all real-valued, infinitely differentiable functions on
\mathbb{R}
whose support is compact.

\Cref

app is dedicated to the proofs of our main results using internal and external lemmas presented in
\Cref
app:auxi_lems and
\Cref
app:Ext_Stats, respectively. Our auxiliary theoretical results are summarized in Table
4
. The dependence of all our results are summarized for the readers’ convenience in
\Cref
fig:dependency_graph.

Appendix B
Proofs

This section includes the proofs of our main results presented in
\Cref
sec:results:
\Cref
thm:charachteriz_funspace,
\Cref
thm:existence_BKL,
\Cref
thm:peeling_all_depth_BKL and
\Cref
thm:excess-risk-BKL.

B.1
Proof of
\Cref
thm:charachteriz_funspace
1

{noheadproof}

Let
f\in\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L)}
be arbitrary. By definition of
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L)}
, there exists a canonical ladder
\mathcal{H}^{(1:L)}_{\hat{\mu}}\in\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(1:L)}
such that
f\in\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(L)}
. By canonical representation, under Assumption
3.1
, for each
l\in[L-1]
there exists
\hat{\mu}^{(l)}\in\mathcal{P}_{1,1}\left(\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(l)}\right)\right)
such that

\displaystyle\hat{k}^{(l)}=k\left[\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(l)}\right),\hat{\mu}^{(l)}\right],\qquad\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(l+1)}=\mathcal{H}_{\hat{k}^{(l)}}.

Hence, without loss of generality, we work with this canonical representation. Applying Lemma
12
at the top layer
L
, one has

\displaystyle|f(\mathbf{x})|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}\|f\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(L)}}\left[\hat{k}^{(L-1)}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x})\right]^{1/2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leq}}\|f\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(L)}}\sqrt[2^{L-1}]{\|\mathbf{x}\|_{2}},\quad\forall\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X},

(36)

where (a) follows from (
81
) and (b) uses Lemma
13
. Similarly,

\displaystyle|f(\mathbf{x})-f(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}\|f\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(L)}}\,d_{\hat{k}^{(L-1)}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leq}}\|f\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(L)}}\sqrt[2^{L-1}]{\|\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\|_{2}},

(37)

for all
\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}
, where (a) follows from (
82
) and (b) uses Lemma
13
. By the definition of
\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f)
,

\displaystyle\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f)=\inf_{\mathcal{H}^{(1:L)}_{\hat{\mu}}\in\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(1:L)}\,:\,f\in\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(L)}}\|f\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(L)}}.

Taking the infimum over all admissible representations in the bounds of (
36
) and (
37
) yields

\displaystyle|f(\mathbf{x})|

\displaystyle\leq\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f)\sqrt[2^{L-1}]{\|\mathbf{x}\|_{2}},

\displaystyle|f(\mathbf{x})-f(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\leq\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f)\sqrt[2^{L-1}]{\|\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\|_{2}},

which proves
\Cref
thm:charachteriz_funspace
1
.

B.2
Proof of
\Cref
thm:charachteriz_funspace
2

{noheadproof}

Let
f\in\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L)}
. We can write simply

\displaystyle\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f)=\inf_{\mathcal{H}^{(1:L)}_{\hat{\mu}}\in\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(1:L)}:\,f\in\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(L)}}\|f\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(L)}},

(38)

where the infimum ranges over all
L
-level BKLs
\mathcal{H}^{(1:L)}_{\hat{\mu}}

realizing
f
at the top layer.
We prove that
\left(\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L)},\widehat{C}^{(L)}\right)
is a quasi-Banach space.

•

Step-1
(vector space structure): Let
f\in\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L)}
, we can choose a
L
-level BKL
\mathcal{H}^{(1:L)}_{\hat{\mu}}\in\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(1:L)}
with

\displaystyle f\in\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(L)},\qquad\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(l+1)}=\mathcal{H}_{\hat{k}^{(l)}},\qquad\hat{k}^{(l)}=k\left[\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(l)}\right),\hat{\mu}^{(l)}\right],\quad\forall\,l\in[L-1].

Scalar multiplication.
Since
\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(L)}
is an RKHS, it is a linear space, hence
cf\in\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(L)}
and

\displaystyle\|cf\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(L)}}=|c|\|f\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(L)}}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\widehat{C}^{(L)}(cf)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}\inf_{\mathcal{H}^{(1:L)}_{\hat{\mu}}\in\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(1:L)}:\,cf\in\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L)}_{\hat{\mu}}}\|cf\|_{\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L)}_{\hat{\mu}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leq}}\|cf\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(L)}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{=}}|c|\|f\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(L)}},

where (a) is the definition of
\widehat{C}^{(L)}
, (b) evaluates the infimum at
\mathcal{H}^{(1:L)}_{\hat{\mu}}
, and (c) uses homogeneity of the RKHS norm. Taking the infimum over all admissible ladders for
f
yields

\displaystyle\widehat{C}^{(L)}(cf)\leq|c|\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f).

(39)

Applying (
39
) to
f=c^{-1}(cf)
gives

\displaystyle\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f)=\widehat{C}^{(L)}\left(c^{-1}cf\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}|c|^{-1}\widehat{C}^{(L)}(cf)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leq}}|c|^{-1}|c|\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f)=\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f),

where (a)–(b) are applications of (
39
). Hence

\displaystyle\widehat{C}^{(L)}(cf)=|c|\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f).

(40)

confirming the property of absolute homogeneity for
\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f)
. Thus,
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L)}
is closed under scalar multiplication.

Addition.
Take
f_{b},f_{c}\in\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L)}
with
\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{b}}^{(1)}=\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{c}}^{(1)}=\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(1)}
and corresponding
L
-level BKLs
\mathcal{H}^{(1:L)}_{\hat{\mu}_{b}}\text{ and }\mathcal{H}^{(1:L)}_{\hat{\mu}_{c}}\in\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(1:L)}
induced by
\hat{\mu}^{(1:L-1)}_{b}\text{ and }\hat{\mu}^{(1:L-1)}_{c}\in\times_{l=1}^{L-1}\mathcal{P}_{1,1}\left(\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(l)}\right)\right)
, respectively. For all
l\in[L-1]
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}_{b}}^{(l+1)}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{H}_{\hat{k}_{b}^{(l)}},\qquad\hat{k}_{b}^{(l)}=k\left[\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{b}}^{(l)}\right),\,\hat{\mu}_{b}^{(l)}\right],

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}_{c}}^{(l+1)}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{H}_{\hat{k}_{c}^{(l)}},\qquad\hat{k}_{c}^{(l)}=k\left[\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{c}}^{(l)}\right),\,\hat{\mu}_{c}^{(l)}\right].

with
f_{b}\in\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}_{b}}^{(L)}
and
f_{c}\in\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}_{c}}^{(L)}
. For each
l\in[L-1]
, define

\displaystyle\mu_{a}^{(l)}\coloneqq\tfrac{1}{2}\hat{\mu}_{b}^{(l)}+\tfrac{1}{2}\hat{\mu}_{c}^{(l)}.

Let
\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{a}}^{(1:L)}
be defined according to
\Cref
def:BKLdef with
\mathcal{S}_{\mu}^{(l)}:=\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{a}}^{(l)}
and
\mu^{(l)}\in\mathcal{P}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{a}}^{(l)}\right)
for all
l\in[L-1]
. One has

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{a}}^{(l+1)}\coloneqq\mathcal{H}_{k_{a}^{(l)}},\quad k_{a}^{(l)}:=k\left[\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{a}}^{(l)},\mu_{a}^{(l)}\right],\quad\text{for all }l\in[L-1].

We aim to prove that
f_{b}+f_{c}\in\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}_{a}}^{(L)}
for the BKL
\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}_{a}}^{(1:L)}\in\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(1:L)}
, after passing to the canonical form of BKL and assuming
\hat{\mu}_{a}^{(1:L-1)}\in\times_{l=1}^{L-1}\mathcal{P}_{1,1}\left(\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(l)}\right)\right)
. Observe that, by the construction of
\mu_{a}^{(l)}
,
\mu_{a}^{(l)}\in\mathcal{P}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{b}}^{(l)}\cup\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{c}}^{(l)}\right)
,

\displaystyle\hat{\mu}_{b}^{(l)}\ll\mu_{a}^{(l)}\quad\text{and}\quad\hat{\mu}_{c}^{(l)}\ll\mu_{a}^{(l)},\quad\text{for all }l\in[L-1],

and for
\mu_{a}^{(l)}
-a.e.
u
,

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}\hat{\mu}_{b}^{(l)}}{\mathrm{d}\mu_{a}^{(l)}}\leq 2\text{ on }\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}_{b}}^{(l)},\qquad\frac{\mathrm{d}\hat{\mu}_{c}^{(l)}}{\mathrm{d}\mu_{a}^{(l)}}\leq 2\text{ on }\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}_{c}}^{(l)},\quad\text{for all }l\in[L-1].

Hence, applying Lemma
18
, gives

\displaystyle\left\|\mu_{a}^{(l)}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{a}}^{(l)},1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{a}}^{(l)}}\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{a}}^{(l)}}\mathrm{d}\mu_{a}^{(l)}(u)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{=}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{a}}^{(l)}}\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{a}}^{(l)}}\mathrm{d}\left(\frac{\mu_{b}^{(l)}+\mu_{c}^{(l)}}{2}\right)(u)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{=}}\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{a}}^{(l)}}\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{a}}^{(l)}}\mathrm{d}\mu_{b}^{(l)}(u)+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{a}}^{(l)}}\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{a}}^{(l)}}\mathrm{d}\mu_{c}^{(l)}(u)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(d)}}{{\leq}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{b}}^{(l)}\right)}\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}_{b}}^{(l)}}\mathrm{d}\hat{\mu}_{b}^{(l)}(u)+\int_{\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{c}}^{(l)}\right)}\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}_{c}}^{(l)}}\mathrm{d}\hat{\mu}_{c}^{(l)}(u)\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(e)}}{{=}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\left\|\hat{\mu}_{b}^{(l)}\right\|_{\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{b}}^{(l)}\right),1}+\left\|\hat{\mu}_{c}^{(l)}\right\|_{\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{c}}^{(l)}\right),1}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(f)}}{{=}}\sqrt{2},

(41)

where (a) is the one moment definition, (b) uses the definition of
\mu_{a}^{(l)}
, (c) a direct application of the linearity of the integral with respect to the measure, (d) follows by applying
\Cref
lem:measures_inequality with
\mathcal{S}_{\mu_{a}}^{(l)}:=\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{a}}^{(l)}
,
\mathcal{S}_{\mu_{b}}^{(l)}:=\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{b}}^{(l)}\right)
. The second term in (c) is handled analogously by another application of
\Cref
lem:measures_inequality with the corresponding substitutions. In (e) we rewrite as one moments, and finally, (f) holds since

\displaystyle\left\|\hat{\mu}_{b}^{(l)}\right\|_{\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{b}}^{(l)}\right),1}=\left\|\hat{\mu}_{c}^{(l)}\right\|_{\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{c}}^{(l)}\right),1}=1.

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\left\|f_{b}+f_{c}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{a}}^{(L)}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}\left\|f_{b}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{a}}^{(L)}}+\left\|f_{c}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{a}}^{(L)}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leq}}\sqrt{2}\left(\left\|f_{b}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{b}}^{(L)}}+\left\|f_{c}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{c}}^{(L)}}\right),

(42)

where (a) is the triangle inequality and (b) comes from
\Cref
lem:measures_inequality. Hence,

\displaystyle C_{1}^{(L)}\left(f_{b}+f_{c}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}\left\|f_{b}+f_{c}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{a}}^{(L)}}\prod_{l=1}^{L-1}\sqrt[2^{L-l}]{\left\|\mu_{a}^{(l)}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{a}}^{(l)},1}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leq}}\frac{2}{\sqrt[2^{L}]{2}}\left(\left\|f_{b}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{b}}^{(L)}}+\left\|f_{c}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{c}}^{(L)}}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{<}}\infty.

(43)

where (a) holds by definition of
C_{1}^{(L)}\left(f_{b}+f_{c}\right)
, (b) uses (
•
‣
B.2
) and (
42
) together with the fact that
\prod_{l=1}^{L-1}\sqrt[2^{L-l}]{\|\mu_{a}^{(l)}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{a}}^{(l)},1}}\leq\sqrt{2}/\sqrt[2^{L}]{2},
which follows from the estimate

\sum_{l=1}^{L-1}2^{-(L-l)}=1/2-1/2^{L}
, and (c) holds since every term in the product and in the top-layer norm is finite by assumption (
f_{b},f_{c}\in\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L)}
), implying
C_{1}^{(L)}(f_{b}+f_{c})<\infty
. Using the canonical form (cf. bullet (2) in the remarks after
\Cref
def:BKLdef), gives
f_{b}+f_{c}\in\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L)}
; which completes the proof that
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L)}
is a vector space.

•

Step-2
(quasi-norm properties):

Positive definiteness.

Let
f\in\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L)}
. Assume that
\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f)=0
. Then for every
\varepsilon>0
there exists
\mathcal{H}^{(1:L)}_{\hat{\mu}}\in\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(1:L)}
such that
f\in\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(l)}
and moreover
\|f\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(L)}}<\varepsilon
. By applying (
17
) of
\Cref
thm:charachteriz_funspace
1
,

\displaystyle|f(\mathbf{x})|\leq\varepsilon\sqrt[2^{L-1}]{\|\mathbf{x}\|_{2}},\quad\forall\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X}.

Letting
\varepsilon\downarrow 0
yields
f=0
. Hence
\widehat{C}^{(L)}
is positive definite.

Absolute homogeneity.

This follows from (
40
) established in Part-1.

Quasi-triangle inequality.
Let
f_{b},f_{c}\in\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L)}
and let
\varepsilon>0
. By definition of
\widehat{C}^{(L)}
, there exist
\mathcal{H}^{(1:L)}_{\hat{\mu}_{b}},\mathcal{H}^{(1:L)}_{\hat{\mu}_{c}}\in\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(1:L)}
such that
f_{b}\in\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}_{b}}^{(L)}
,
f_{c}\in\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}_{c}}^{(L)}
, and additionally

\displaystyle\|f_{b}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}_{b}}^{(L)}}\leq\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f_{b})+\varepsilon,\qquad\|f_{c}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}_{c}}^{(L)}}\leq\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f_{c})+\varepsilon.

(44)

Applying (
•
‣
B.2
) to these representations gives

\displaystyle\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f_{b}+f_{c})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}\frac{2}{\sqrt[2^{L}]{2}}\left(\|f_{b}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}_{b}}^{(L)}}+\|f_{c}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}_{c}}^{(L)}}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leq}}\frac{2}{\sqrt[2^{L}]{2}}\left(\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f_{b})+\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f_{c})+2\varepsilon\right),

where (a) follows from (
•
‣
B.2
), and (b) uses (
44
). Letting
\varepsilon\downarrow 0
yields

\displaystyle\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f_{b}+f_{c})\leq\frac{2}{\sqrt[2^{L}]{2}}\left(\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f_{b})+\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f_{c})\right).

(45)

Thus
\widehat{C}^{(L)}
satisfies a quasi-triangle inequality with constant
K=2/\sqrt[2^{L}]{2}
. This proves that
\widehat{C}^{(L)}
is a quasi-norm on
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L)}
.

•

Step-3
(completeness): To establish completeness, we need to show that
\left(\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L)},\widehat{C}^{(L)}\right)
is complete with respect to the quasi-norm
\widehat{C}^{(L)}
.
Let
(f_{j})_{j\geq 1}
be a Cauchy sequence in
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L)}
. That is, for every
\varepsilon>0
, there exists
N_{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{Z}^{>0}
such that

\displaystyle\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f_{n_{1}}-f_{n_{2}})<\varepsilon,\qquad\text{for all }n_{1},n_{2}>N_{\varepsilon}.

Then we define

\displaystyle h_{0}\coloneqq f_{n_{1}},\quad\text{and}\quad h_{j}\coloneqq f_{n_{j+1}}-f_{n_{j}},\quad\text{for each}\,\,j\in\mathbb{Z}^{>0},

It follows that
\forall j\in\mathbb{Z}^{>0}

\displaystyle\widehat{C}^{(L)}(h_{j})=\widehat{C}^{(L)}\left(f_{n_{j+1}}-f_{n_{j}}\right)<\epsilon_{j},\quad n_{j}\coloneqq\ceil*{N_{\epsilon_{j}}},\,\,\epsilon_{j}\coloneqq 2^{-(j+1)L},

(46)

by construction. Then,
\forall j\in\mathbb{Z}^{>0}

\exists\,\mathcal{H}^{(l:L)}_{\hat{\mu}_{j}}\in\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(1:L)}
induced by
\hat{\mu}^{(1:L-1)}_{j}
such that

\displaystyle\hat{k}_{j}^{(l)}=k\left[\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{j}}^{(l)}\right),\,\hat{\mu}_{j}^{(l)}\right],\quad\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}_{j}}^{(l+1)}=\mathcal{H}_{\hat{k}_{j}^{(l)}},\quad h_{j}\in\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}_{j}}^{(L)},\quad\widehat{C}^{(L)}(h_{j})=\|h_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}_{j}}^{(L)}},

for all
l\in[L-1]
. Notice that we have

\displaystyle|f_{j}(\mathbf{x})-f_{j^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x})|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}|(f_{j}-f_{j^{\prime}})(\mathbf{x})|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leq}}\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f_{j}-f_{j^{\prime}})\sqrt[2^{L-1}]{\mathbf{\|}\mathbf{x}\|_{2}},\quad\forall j,j^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{>0},\,\forall\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X},

where (a) holds by the linearity of function evaluation and (b) uses (
17
) of
\Cref
thm:charachteriz_funspace
1
. Since
(f_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
is Cauchy in
\widehat{C}^{(L)}
,
(f_{j}(x))_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
is Cauchy in
\mathbb{R}
, hence

\displaystyle f_{\infty}(x):=\lim_{j\to\infty}f_{j}(x)\quad\text{exists for each }x\in\mathcal{X}.

It is sufficient to prove that
f_{\infty}\in\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L)}
. To this purpose, for each
l\in[L-1]
, we set

\displaystyle\mu_{s}^{(l)}\coloneqq(2^{l}-1)\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-(j+1)l}\hat{\mu}_{j}^{(l)},\quad\forall l\in[L-1].

(47)

Using
\Cref
lemma:TVconvergence
1
with
\mathcal{S}_{\mu_{j}}^{(l)}:=\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{j}}^{(l)}\right)
for each
l\in[L-1]
, we have
\mu_{s}^{(l)}\in\mathcal{P}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}_{j}}^{(l)}\right)
. Hence, it holds for each
l\in[L-2]
and
j\in\mathbb{Z}^{>0}
that

\displaystyle\left\|\mu_{s}^{(l+1)}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{s}}^{(l+1)},1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}\int_{\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{s}}^{(l+1)}}\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{s}}^{(l+1)}}\mathrm{d}\mu_{s}^{(l+1)}(u)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{=}}(2^{l+1}-1)\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-(j+1)(l+1)}\int_{\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{j}}^{(l+1)}\right)}\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{s}}^{(l+1)}}\mathrm{d}\hat{\mu}_{j}^{(l+1)}(u)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{\leq}}(2^{l+1}-1)\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-(j+1)(l+1)}\sqrt{\frac{2^{(j+1)l}}{2^{l}-1}}\int_{\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{j}}^{(l+1)}\right)}\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}_{j}}^{(l+1)}}\mathrm{d}\hat{\mu}_{j}^{(l+1)}(u)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(d)}}{{\leq}}\frac{2^{l+1}-1}{2^{l}-1}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-(j+1)(l+1)}2^{(j+1)l}\int_{\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{j}}^{(l+1)}\right)}\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}_{j}}^{(l+1)}}\mathrm{d}\hat{\mu}_{j}^{(l+1)}(u)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(e)}}{{=}}\frac{2^{l+1}-1}{2^{l}-1}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-(j+1)}\|\mu_{j}^{(l+1)}\|_{\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{j}}^{(l+1)}\right),1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(f)}}{{=}}\frac{2^{l+1}-1}{2^{l}-1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(g)}}{{<}}3.

Here (a) corresponds to the definition of the first moment,
(b) is obtained from (
47
) together with linearity of the integral with respect to
\mu_{s}^{(l+1)}
,
(c) invokes
\Cref
lemma:TVconvergence
2
with
\mathcal{S}_{\mu_{j}}^{(l)}=\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{j}}^{(l)}\right)
for all
l\in\left[L-1\right]
,
(d) exploits elementary inequalities to separate the factors,
(e) corresponds again to the definition of the first moment,
(f) combines
\sum_{j\geq 0}2^{-\left(j+1\right)}=1
with the normalization
\left\|\mu_{j}^{(l+1)}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{j}}^{(l+1)},1}=1
,
and (g) relies on the fact that the ratio is uniformly bounded. Consequently, for all
l\in\left[L-1\right]
, one has
\left\|\mu_{s}^{(l+1)}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{s}}^{(l+1)},1}<\infty
. Using
\Cref
assumption:normalizing and passing to the canonical form of BKL, enable us to define a
L
-level BKL
\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}_{s}}^{(1:L)}\in\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(1:L)}
such that
\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}_{s}}^{(l+1)}\coloneqq\mathcal{H}_{\hat{k}^{(l)}}
and
\hat{k}^{(l)}=k\left[\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu_{s}}^{(l)}\right),\hat{\mu}_{s}^{(l)}\right]
for each
l\in[L-1]
. All in all,

\displaystyle\|f_{\infty}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}_{s}}^{(L)}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}\|\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}h_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}_{s}}^{(L)}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leq}}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\|h_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}_{s}}^{(L)}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{\leq}}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\sqrt{\frac{2^{(j+1)(L-1)}}{2^{L-1}-1}}\|h_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}_{j}}^{(L)}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(d)}}{{=}}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\sqrt{\frac{2^{(j+1)(L-1)}}{2^{L-1}-1}}\widehat{C}^{(L)}(h_{j})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(e)}}{{=}}\sqrt{\frac{2^{L-1}}{2^{L-1}-1}}\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f_{n_{1}})+\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\sqrt{\frac{2^{(j+1)(L-1)}}{2^{L-1}-1}}\widehat{C}^{(L)}(h_{j})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(f)}}{{\leq}}\sqrt{\frac{2^{L-1}}{2^{L-1}-1}}\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f_{n_{1}})+\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\sqrt{\frac{2^{(j+1)(L-1)}}{2^{L-1}-1}}2^{-(j+1)L}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(g)}}{{\leq}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2^{L-1}-1}}\left(\sqrt{2^{L-1}}\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f_{n_{1}})+\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}2^{(j+1)(L-1)}2^{-(j+1)L}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(h)}}{{\leq}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2^{L-1}-1}}\left(\sqrt{2^{L-1}}\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f_{n_{1}})+\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}2^{-(j+1)}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{<}}\infty.

Here (a) follows from the definition of
f_{\infty}
,
(b) applies the triangle inequality,
(c) invokes
\Cref
lemma:TVconvergence
2
,
(d) uses the identity
\widehat{C}^{(L)}(h_{j})=\|h_{j}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}_{j}}^{(L)}}
,
(e) separates the first term of the series,
(f) applies the bound
\widehat{C}^{(L)}(h_{j})\leq 2^{-(j+1)L}
,
(g) factors out the common term and simplifies the exponents,
(h) uses the identity
2^{(j+1)(L-1)}2^{-(j+1)L}=2^{-(j+1)}
,
and (i) holds since
\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f_{n_{1}})<\infty
and
\sum_{j\geq 1}2^{-(j+1)}<\infty
. Finally, we show
f_{j}\to f_{\infty}
in
\widehat{C}^{(L)}
. For the subsequence
(n_{m})_{m}
,
by the quasi-triangle inequality with constant
K=\sqrt[2^{L}]{2}
,

\displaystyle\widehat{C}^{(L)}\left(f_{n_{m}}-f_{\infty}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}\widehat{C}^{(L)}\left(\sum_{j=m}^{\infty}h_{j}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leq}}K\sum_{j=m}^{\infty}\widehat{C}^{(L)}\left(h_{j}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{\leq}}K\sum_{j=m}^{\infty}2^{-(j+1)L}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(d)}}{{\longrightarrow}}0,\qquad m\to\infty.

Here (a) follows from the definition
f_{\infty}=\sum_{j\geq 0}h_{j}
,
(b) uses the quasi-triangle inequality of
\widehat{C}^{(L)}
with constant
K
,
(c) follows from the bound
\widehat{C}^{(L)}\left(h_{j}\right)\leq 2^{-(j+1)L}
,
and (d) holds since the tail of a convergent geometric series vanishes.
Since
(f_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
is Cauchy, the whole sequence converges to
f_{\infty}
in
\widehat{C}^{(L)}
.
Therefore
\left(\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L)},\widehat{C}^{(L)}\right)
is complete.

B.3
Proof of
\Cref
thm:charachteriz_funspace
3

{noheadproof}

•

Inclusion
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L)}\subseteq\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L+1)}
: Let
f^{(L)}\in\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L)}
. For all
l\in[L-1]
,
\exists\hat{\mu}^{(l)}\in\mathcal{P}_{1,1}\left(\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(l)}\right)\right)
such that

\displaystyle k^{(l)}=k\left[\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(l)}\right),\hat{\mu}^{(l)}\right],\qquad\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(l+1)}=\mathcal{H}_{\hat{k}^{(l)}},\quad f^{(L)}\in\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(L)}.

(48)

Fix
\varepsilon>0
and choose such a representation so that

\displaystyle\left\|f^{(L)}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(L)}}\leq\widehat{C}^{(L)}\left(f^{(L)}\right)+\varepsilon.

Define the normalized element

\displaystyle\tilde{u}_{0}:=\frac{f^{(L)}}{\left\|f^{(L)}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(L)}}}\in\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(L)}\right),

and set the
L
-layer measure to be
\hat{\mu}^{(L)}:=\delta_{\tilde{u}_{0}}
. Then

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(L)}\right)}\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(L)}}\mathrm{d}\hat{\mu}^{(L)}(u)=\|\tilde{u}_{0}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(L)}}=1,

hence
\hat{\mu}^{(L)}\in\mathcal{P}_{1,1}\left(\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(L)}\right)\right)
. We thus obtain an
(L{+}1)
-level BKL
\mathcal{H}^{(1:L+1)}_{\hat{\mu}}
by keeping the first
L
layers unchanged and appending
\hat{\mu}^{(L)}
. By Lemma
15
, each element of
\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(L+1)}
has the form

\displaystyle f^{(L+1)}(\mathbf{x})=\int_{\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(L)}\right)}h_{u}^{(L)}\left(u(\mathbf{x})\right)\mathrm{d}\hat{\mu}^{(L)}(u)=h_{\tilde{u}_{0}}^{(L)}\left(\tilde{u}_{0}(\mathbf{x})\right),\qquad h_{\tilde{u}_{0}}^{(L)}\in\mathcal{H}_{k^{(\mathrm{B})}}.

(49)

To reproduce
f^{(L)}
itself, we require
h_{\tilde{u}_{0}}^{(L)}\circ\tilde{u}_{0}=f^{(L)}
. Since
\mathcal{X}
is compact and
\tilde{u}_{0}\in\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X})
, its range is bounded:

\displaystyle R:=\sup_{x\in\mathcal{X}}|\tilde{u}_{0}(\mathbf{x})|<\infty.

(50)

Choose
\phi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
such that
\phi(0)=0
and
\phi(t)=t
for all
|t|\leq R
. Then
\phi
is absolutely continuous and
\phi^{\prime}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
, hence
\phi\in\mathcal{H}_{k^{(\mathrm{B})}}
(by
\Cref
lem:brownian-rkhs). Define

\displaystyle h_{\tilde{u}_{0}}^{(L)}\coloneqq\left\|f^{(L)}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(L)}}\,\phi\in\mathcal{H}_{k^{(\mathrm{B})}}.

(51)

Since
\phi(t)=t
on
[-R,R]
and
\tilde{u}_{0}(\mathcal{X})\subseteq[-R,R]
, we have for every
\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X}
,

\displaystyle h_{\tilde{u}_{0}}^{(L)}\left(\tilde{u}_{0}(\mathbf{x})\right)=\left\|f^{(L)}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(L)}}\,\phi\left(\tilde{u}_{0}(\mathbf{x})\right)=\left\|f^{(L)}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(L)}}\,\tilde{u}_{0}(\mathbf{x})=f^{(L)}(\mathbf{x}).

Moreover, since
\phi\in\mathcal{H}_{k^{(\mathrm{B})}}
and
h=\|f^{(L)}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(L)}}\,\phi
, we have

\displaystyle\|h_{\tilde{u}_{0}}^{(L)}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{k^{(\mathrm{B})}}}\leq\|f^{(L)}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(L)}}\cdot\|\phi\|_{\mathcal{H}_{k^{(\mathrm{B})}}}.

Since
\phi
depends only on the compact range
[-R,R]
, its RKHS norm is bounded by a constant depending only on
R
, hence on
\mathcal{X}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\|f^{(L)}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(L+1)}}\leq C\|f^{(L)}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(L)}}.

Taking infimum over all realizations yields

\displaystyle\mathit{C}^{(L+1)}(f)\leq C\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f).

Combining with (
49
) shows that
f^{(L)}\in\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(L+1)}
, hence
f^{(L)}\in\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L+1)}
. Therefore
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L)}\subseteq\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L+1)}
.

•

Strict inclusion
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L)}\subsetneq\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L+1)}
:
We already know from
the part-1
that
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L)}\subseteq\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L+1)}
. It remains to prove strictness.

Step-1
(Brownian nonlinearity): Fix

\displaystyle a\in\left(\frac{1}{2},\,2^{-(L-1)/L}\right).

Let
\eta\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
satisfy

\displaystyle 0\leq\eta\leq 1,\qquad\eta(t)=1\text{ for }|t|\leq\tfrac{1}{2}.

Define

\displaystyle t_{+}:=\max\{t,0\},\qquad g_{a}(t):=\eta(t)\,(t_{+})^{a}.

Then
g_{a}(0)=0
and, for
t>0
sufficiently small,
g_{a}^{\prime}(t)=at^{a-1}
, so

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}|g_{a}^{\prime}(t)|^{2}dt<\infty.

By
\Cref
lem:brownian-rkhs,
g_{a}\in\mathcal{H}_{k^{(\mathrm{B})}}
.

Step-2
(recursive construction): Define recursively

\displaystyle u_{l+1}:=g_{a}\circ u_{l}.

We show by induction that
u_{l+1}\in\mathcal{H}^{(l+1)}
. Assume
u_{l}\in\mathcal{H}^{(l)}
.
If
u_{l}\equiv 0
, then
u_{l+1}\equiv 0\in\mathcal{H}^{(l+1)}
.
Otherwise define

\displaystyle\tilde{u}_{l}:=\frac{u_{l}}{\|u_{l}\|},\qquad\hat{\mu}^{(l)}:=\delta_{\tilde{u}_{l}},

and
h_{l}(s):=g_{a}(\|u_{l}\|\,s)
. Then
h_{l}\in\mathcal{H}_{k^{(\mathrm{B})}}
and

\displaystyle h_{l}(\tilde{u}_{l}(\mathbf{x}))=g_{a}(u_{l}(\mathbf{x}))=u_{l+1}(\mathbf{x}).

By
\Cref
coro:IRKHS_BKL, this implies
u_{l+1}\in\mathcal{H}^{(l+1)}
. Thus

\displaystyle u_{L+1}\in\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L+1)}.

(52)

Step-3
(local power bounds along the trace): We claim that there exist
\delta>0
and constants
c_{l},C_{l}>0
such that

\displaystyle c_{l}\,t^{a^{l-1}}\leq u_{l}(\gamma(t))\leq C_{l}\,t^{a^{l-1}}\qquad(0\leq t\leq\delta),

(53)

for all
l=1,\dots,L+1
. We prove this by induction on
l
. For
l=1
, this is exactly the assumption. Assume the claim holds for some
l\leq L
. Since
a>0
, we have

\displaystyle u_{l}(\gamma(t))\to 0\qquad(t\downarrow 0),

so, after possibly shrinking
\delta
, we may assume

\displaystyle 0\leq u_{l}(\gamma(t))\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\qquad(0\leq t\leq\delta).

Hence
\eta\equiv 1
on the relevant range, and therefore

\displaystyle u_{l+1}(\gamma(t))=g_{a}(u_{l}(\gamma(t)))=u_{l}(\gamma(t))^{a}.

Using the induction hypothesis,

\displaystyle c_{l}^{a}t^{a^{l}}\leq u_{l+1}(\gamma(t))\leq C_{l}^{a}t^{a^{l}},

which proves (
53
) for
l+1
. In particular, for
l=L+1
, there exists
c>0
such that

\displaystyle u_{L+1}(\gamma(t))\geq c\,t^{a^{L}}\qquad(0\leq t\leq\delta).

(54)

Also, since
u_{1}(\mathbf{x}_{0})=0
and
g_{a}(0)=0
, we have

\displaystyle u_{l}(\mathbf{x}_{0})=0\quad\text{for all }l,\qquad\text{hence}\qquad u_{L+1}(\mathbf{x}_{0})=0.

(55)

Step-4
(Hölder contradiction): Suppose that
u_{L+1}\in\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L)}
.
Then by applying (
16
) of

\Cref
thm:charachteriz_funspace
1
,

u_{L+1}
is
2^{-(L-1)}
-Hölder, i.e.,

\displaystyle|u_{L+1}(\mathbf{x})-u_{L+1}(\mathbf{y})|\leq\|\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y}\|_{2}^{2^{-(L-1)}}\qquad\text{for all }\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}\in\mathcal{X}.

Applying this with
\mathbf{x}=\gamma(t)
and
\mathbf{y}=\mathbf{x}_{0}
, and using that
\gamma
is
Lipschitz, we obtain

\displaystyle u_{L+1}(\gamma(t))=|u_{L+1}(\gamma(t))-u_{L+1}(\mathbf{x}_{0})|\leq C^{\prime}t^{2^{-(L-1)}}

for some constant
C^{\prime}>0
. Combining this with (
54
) yields

\displaystyle c\,t^{a^{L}}\leq C^{\prime}t^{2^{-(L-1)}}.

Since
a^{L}<2^{-(L-1)}
, we have

\displaystyle t^{a^{L}-2^{-(L-1)}}\to\infty\qquad(t\downarrow 0),

a contradiction. Hence

\displaystyle u_{L+1}\notin\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L)}.

(56)

From (
52
) and (
56
),
u_{L+1}\in\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L+1)}\setminus\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L)}
, so

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L)}\subsetneq\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L+1)}.

Step-4
(ballwise separation):
Define recursively

\displaystyle u_{l+1}:=g_{a}\circ u_{l},\qquad l=1,\dots,L,

and set
f_{\star}:=u_{L+1}
. Hence
f_{\star}\in\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L+1)}\setminus\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L)}
. There exist
\rho_{\star}>0
and constants
c_{\star},C_{\star}>0
such that

\displaystyle f_{\star}(\mathbf{x}_{0})=0,\qquad c_{\star}t^{a^{L}}\leq f_{\star}(\gamma(t))\leq C_{\star}t^{a^{L}},\qquad t\in[0,\rho_{\star}].

Fix
r>0
. Let

\displaystyle a

\displaystyle\in\left(\tfrac{1}{2},\,2^{-\left(L-1\right)/L}\right),

\displaystyle\alpha_{L}

\displaystyle\coloneqq 2^{-\left(L-1\right)},

\displaystyle\beta_{L}

\displaystyle\coloneqq a^{L},

\displaystyle K_{\gamma}

\displaystyle\coloneqq L_{\gamma}^{\alpha_{L}},

where
L_{\gamma}
denotes the Lipschitz constant of
\gamma
on
[0,\rho_{\star}]
.
Since
\beta_{L}<\alpha_{L}
, we can choose
t_{r}\in\left(0,\rho_{\star}\right]
such that

\displaystyle rK_{\gamma}t_{r}^{\alpha_{L}}\leq\frac{c_{\star}}{4}t_{r}^{\beta_{L}}.

(57)

Indeed, rearranging the inequality gives

\displaystyle t_{r}^{\alpha_{L}-\beta_{L}}\leq\frac{c_{\star}}{4rK_{\gamma}}.

Since
\alpha_{L}-\beta_{L}>0
, this is ensured by taking

\displaystyle t_{r}\coloneqq\min\left\{\rho_{\star},\,\left(\frac{c_{\star}}{4rK_{\gamma}}\right)^{\frac{1}{\alpha_{L}-\beta_{L}}}\right\}.

Let
g\in\mathcal{B}^{(L)}_{r}
be arbitrary. Applying the Hölder estimate
(
16
) with
\mathbf{x}=\gamma(t_{r})
and
\mathbf{x}^{\prime}=\mathbf{x}_{0}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|g\left(\gamma(t_{r})\right)-g\left(\mathbf{x}_{0}\right)\right|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}\widehat{C}^{(L)}\left(g\right)\left\|\gamma(t_{r})-\mathbf{x}_{0}\right\|_{2}^{\alpha_{L}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leq}}r\left\|\gamma(t_{r})-\mathbf{x}_{0}\right\|_{2}^{\alpha_{L}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{\leq}}rK_{\gamma}t_{r}^{\alpha_{L}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(d)}}{{\leq}}\frac{c_{\star}}{4}t_{r}^{\beta_{L}}.

(58)

Here (a) follows from the Hölder estimate (
16
) of
\Cref
thm:charachteriz_funspace
1
, (b) uses the definition of
\mathcal{B}^{(L)}_{r}
, (c) comes from the Lipschitz property of
\gamma
, and (d) is ensured by the choice of
t_{r}
in (
57
). We now distinguish two cases.

Case 1.
Assume

\displaystyle\left|g\left(\mathbf{x}_{0}\right)\right|\geq\frac{c_{\star}}{4}t_{r}^{\beta_{L}}.

Since
f_{\star}(\mathbf{x}_{0})=0
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|f_{\star}-g\right\|_{\infty}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\geq}}\left|f_{\star}(\mathbf{x}_{0})-g(\mathbf{x}_{0})\right|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{=}}\left|g(\mathbf{x}_{0})\right|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{\geq}}\frac{c_{\star}}{4}t_{r}^{\beta_{L}}.

Here (a) follows from the definition of the supremum norm, (b) uses
f_{\star}(\mathbf{x}_{0})=0
, and (c) uses the case assumption.

Case 2.
Assume

\displaystyle\left|g\left(\mathbf{x}_{0}\right)\right|<\frac{c_{\star}}{4}t_{r}^{\beta_{L}}.

Then

\displaystyle\left|g\left(\gamma(t_{r})\right)\right|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}\left|g\left(\mathbf{x}_{0}\right)\right|+\left|g\left(\gamma(t_{r})\right)-g\left(\mathbf{x}_{0}\right)\right|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{<}}\frac{c_{\star}}{4}t_{r}^{\beta_{L}}+\frac{c_{\star}}{4}t_{r}^{\beta_{L}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{=}}\frac{c_{\star}}{2}t_{r}^{\beta_{L}}.

Here (a) follows from the triangle inequality, (b) combines the case assumption with (
58
), and (c) simplifies the sum. On the other hand, from the definition of the witness
f_{\star}
,

\displaystyle f_{\star}\left(\gamma(t_{r})\right)\geq c_{\star}t_{r}^{\beta_{L}}.

Hence

\displaystyle\left\|f_{\star}-g\right\|_{\infty}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\geq}}\left|f_{\star}\left(\gamma(t_{r})\right)-g\left(\gamma(t_{r})\right)\right|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\geq}}c_{\star}t_{r}^{\beta_{L}}-\frac{c_{\star}}{2}t_{r}^{\beta_{L}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{=}}\frac{c_{\star}}{2}t_{r}^{\beta_{L}}.

Here (a) uses the definition of the supremum norm, (b) combines the lower bound on
f_{\star}
with the estimate on
g
, and (c) simplifies the expression. In both cases we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|f_{\star}-g\right\|_{\infty}\geq\frac{c_{\star}}{4}t_{r}^{\beta_{L}}.

Since
g\in\mathcal{B}^{(L)}_{r}
was arbitrary, it follows that

\displaystyle\inf_{g\in\mathcal{B}^{(L)}_{r}}\left\|f_{\star}-g\right\|_{\infty}\geq\frac{c_{\star}}{4}t_{r}^{\beta_{L}}>0,

which proves the claim.

B.4
Proof of
\Cref
thm:existence_BKL
1

{noheadproof}

Let
(f_{m})_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L)}
be a minimizing sequence for the regularized empirical
objective
J_{n}(f)=\mathcal{R}_{n}(f)+\lambda\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f)
, where

\mathcal{R}_{n}(f)=\tfrac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell(y_{i},f(x_{i}))
and

\ell(y,\cdot)
is continuous and nonnegative. We have

\displaystyle\lambda\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f_{m})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}J_{n}(f_{m})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leq}}\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}J_{n}(f_{k})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{<}}\infty.

(59)

Here (a) uses
\mathcal{R}_{n}(f_{m})\geq 0
(since
\ell(y,\cdot)\geq 0
), hence

\lambda\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f_{m})\leq\lambda\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f_{m})+\mathcal{R}_{n}(f_{m})=J_{n}(f_{m})
, (b) is trivial because each term is bounded by the supremum, and (c) holds since
(f_{m})
is a minimizing sequence, hence
(J_{n}(f_{m}))_{m\in\mathbb{N}}

is bounded above (after discarding finitely many initial terms if needed). Lemma
31
implies that
\mathcal{B}^{(L)}_{r}
is relatively compact in

(\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X}),\|\cdot\|_{\infty})
, hence (after passing to a subsequence, not
relabeled)
f_{m}\to f
uniformly on
\mathcal{X}
for some
f\in\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X})
.
By lower semicontinuity of the quasi-norm
(Lemma
30
),

\displaystyle\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f)\leq\liminf_{m\to\infty}\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f_{m})\leq r<\infty.

(60)

So,
f\in\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L)}
. Hence

\displaystyle J_{n}(f)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}_{n}(f)+\lambda\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}\lim_{m\to\infty}\mathcal{R}_{n}(f_{m})+\lambda\liminf_{m\to\infty}\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f_{m})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leq}}\liminf_{m\to\infty}\left(\mathcal{R}_{n}(f_{m})+\lambda\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f_{m})\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{=}}\inf_{g\in\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L)}}J_{n}(g),

where (a) uses (
60
) and the fact that

f_{m}\to f
uniformly on
\mathcal{X}
, which implies

\mathcal{R}_{n}(f_{m})\to\mathcal{R}_{n}(f)
since
\ell(y_{i},\cdot)
is continuous for each
i\in[n]
; (b) follow from the fact that

\lim a_{m}+\liminf b_{m}\leq\liminf(a_{m}+b_{m})
, and (c) holds because
(f_{m})_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
is a minimizing sequence. Thus
f
attains the minimum:
\hat{f}_{\lambda}=f
.

B.5
Proof of
\Cref
thm:existence_BKL
8

{noheadproof}

Assume that
\ell(y,\cdot)
is strictly convex for every
y\in\mathcal{Y}
.
Let
f,g\in\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L)}
be two minimizers of
\mathcal{R}
. Then for any
t\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}(tf+(1-t)g)\leq t\,\mathcal{R}(f)+(1-t)\,\mathcal{R}(g)=\mathcal{R}^{*}.

By minimality,
tf+(1-t)g
is also a minimizer. The strict convexity of
\ell(y,\cdot)
implies that if

f\neq g
on a set of positive
\nu_{X}
-measure, then

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}(tf+(1-t)g)<t\,\mathcal{R}(f)+(1-t)\,\mathcal{R}(g),

which is impossible. Hence
f=g

\nu_{X}
-almost everywhere. Therefore any minimizer of
\mathcal{R}
over
\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L)}
, if it exists, is unique up to
\nu_{X}
-almost everywhere equality.

B.6
Proof of
\Cref
thm:peeling_all_depth_BKL

{noheadproof}

Let
f\in\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L)}
and
r>0
. Then, for each
l\in[L-1]
there exists a probability measure
\hat{\mu}^{(l)}\in\mathcal{P}_{1,1}\left(\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(l)}\right)\right)
satisfying

\displaystyle\hat{k}^{(l)}=k\left[\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(l)}\right),\hat{\mu}^{(l)}\right],\qquad\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(l+1)}=\mathcal{H}_{\hat{k}^{(l)}},\quad f\in\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(L)}.

In particular,

\displaystyle\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f)=\inf_{\mathcal{H}^{(1:L)}_{\hat{\mu}}\in\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(1:L)}\,:\,f\in\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(L)}}\|f\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(L)}},\qquad\forall f\in\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L)}.

Throughout the proof we fix the sample
(X_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
and take expectations only with respect to the Gaussian variables unless stated otherwise.
Let
\bm{\varepsilon}=(\varepsilon_{i})_{i=1}^{n}\sim\mathcal{N}^{n}(0,1)
. By
\Cref
lem:simplified_gaussian_complexity, it suffices to control
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{n}\left(\mathcal{B}^{(L)}_{1}\right)
.
Using the representation obtained in the proof of

\Cref
lem:simplified_gaussian_complexity, we have

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{n}\left(\mathcal{B}^{(L)}_{1}\right)=\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}^{n}(0,1)}\left[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\|g\|_{\mathcal{H}_{k^{(\mathrm{B})}}}\leq 1\\
\hat{\mathit{C}}^{(L-1)}(u)\leq 1\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{i}\,g\left(u(\mathbf{x}_{i})\right)\right].

(61)

By
\Cref
lem:bach18_BKL, for each fixed
u
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\|g\|_{\mathcal{H}_{k^{(\mathrm{B})}}}\leq 1}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{i}\,g\left(u(X_{i})\right)=\frac{1}{n}\sqrt{\bm{\varepsilon}^{\top}\textbf{K}_{u}^{\textbf{(B)}}\bm{\varepsilon}},

Substituting this into (
61
) yields

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{n}\left(\mathcal{B}^{(L)}_{1}\right)=\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}^{n}(0,1)}\left[\sup_{\widehat{\mathit{C}}^{(L-1)}(u)\leq 1}\frac{1}{n}\sqrt{\bm{\varepsilon}^{\top}\textbf{K}_{u}^{\textbf{(B)}}\bm{\varepsilon}}\right].

(62)

Let
R_{X}:=\max_{i\in[n]}\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|_{2}
. Let

\displaystyle\alpha:=2^{-(L-2)},\qquad\Gamma_{T}(\mathbf{x}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{x}_{n}):=\sum_{\{i,j\}\in E_{T}}\|\mathbf{x}_{i}-\mathbf{x}_{j}\|_{2}^{\alpha}+2R_{X}^{\alpha}.

Let
T=([n],E_{T})
be any tree on
[n]
, and let
\bm{\varepsilon}=(\varepsilon_{1},\dots,\varepsilon_{n})\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)^{n}
.
For any
B\subset[n]
, define

\displaystyle\varepsilon(B):=\sum_{i\in B}\varepsilon_{i}.

For any
\mathbf{a}=(a_{1},\dots,a_{n})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
t\geq 0
, define

\displaystyle A_{t}^{+}(\mathbf{a}):=\{i\in[n]:a_{i}\geq t\},\qquad A_{t}^{-}(\mathbf{a}):=\{i\in[n]:a_{i}\leq-t\}.

For any
B\subset[n]
, define its tree boundary size by

\displaystyle\partial_{T}(B):=\sum_{\{i,j\}\in E_{T}}\bigl|\mathbf{1}_{B}(i)-\mathbf{1}_{B}(j)\bigr|.

For any
\mathbf{a}=(a_{1},\dots,a_{n})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, define its tree total variation by

\displaystyle\mathrm{TV}_{T}(\mathbf{a}):=\sum_{\{i,j\}\in E_{T}}|a_{i}-a_{j}|.

Fix
u\in\mathcal{H}^{(L-1)}_{\hat{\mu}}
and define

\displaystyle\mathbf{a}(u):=(u(\mathbf{x}_{1}),\dots,u(\mathbf{x}_{n}))\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

By the Brownian threshold lemma (
\Cref
lem:brownian-threshold-rigorous),

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\top}\mathbf{K}_{u}^{(B)}\varepsilon=\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\varepsilon\big(A_{t}^{+}(\mathbf{a}(u))\big)^{2}+\varepsilon\big(A_{t}^{-}(\mathbf{a}(u))\big)^{2}\right)\,dt.

(63)

Define

\displaystyle S_{T}(\varepsilon):=\sup_{B\subset[n]}\frac{\varepsilon(B)^{2}}{\partial_{T}(B)\vee 1}.

Then for every
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\varepsilon\big(A_{t}^{\pm}(\mathbf{a}(u))\big)^{2}\leq S_{T}(\varepsilon)\bigl(\partial_{T}(A_{t}^{\pm}(\mathbf{a}(u)))\vee 1\bigr),

and hence

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\top}\mathbf{K}_{u}^{(B)}\varepsilon\leq S_{T}(\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{\infty}\left((\partial_{T}(A_{t}^{+}(\mathbf{a}(u)))\vee 1)+(\partial_{T}(A_{t}^{-}(\mathbf{a}(u)))\vee 1)\right)\,dt.

(64)

By the coarea identity with boundary correction (
\Cref
lem:coarea-correction-full),

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\left((\partial_{T}(A_{t}^{+}(\mathbf{a}(u)))\vee 1)+(\partial_{T}(A_{t}^{-}(\mathbf{a}(u)))\vee 1)\right)\,dt\leq\mathrm{TV}_{T}(\mathbf{a}(u))+2\|\mathbf{a}(u)\|_{\infty}.

(65)

Combining (
64
) and (
65
),

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\top}\mathbf{K}_{u}^{(B)}\varepsilon\leq S_{T}(\varepsilon)\Bigl(\mathrm{TV}_{T}(\mathbf{a}(u))+2\|\mathbf{a}(u)\|_{\infty}\Bigr).

(66)

Using (
16
) from
\Cref
thm:charachteriz_funspace, we have

\displaystyle|u(\mathbf{x}_{i})-u(\mathbf{x}_{j})|\leq\|\mathbf{x}_{i}-\mathbf{x}_{j}\|^{\alpha},

hence

\displaystyle\mathrm{TV}_{T}(\mathbf{a}(u))=\sum_{\{i,j\}\in E_{T}}|u(\mathbf{x}_{i})-u(\mathbf{x}_{j})|\leq\sum_{\{i,j\}\in E_{T}}\|\mathbf{x}_{i}-\mathbf{x}_{j}\|^{\alpha}.

(67)

Second, applying (
17
) from
\Cref
thm:charachteriz_funspace, yields

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{a}(u)\|_{\infty}=\max_{i\in[n]}|u(\mathbf{x}_{i})|\leq R_{X}^{\alpha}.

(68)

Substituting (
67
) and (
68
) into (
66
),

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\top}\mathbf{K}_{u}^{(B)}\varepsilon\leq S_{T}(\varepsilon)\,\Gamma_{T}(\mathbf{x}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{x}_{n}).

(69)

Taking supremum over
u
and square roots,

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathit{C}^{(L-1)}\leq 1}\sqrt{\varepsilon^{\top}\mathbf{K}_{u}^{(B)}\varepsilon}\leq\sqrt{S_{T}(\varepsilon)}\,\Gamma_{T}(\mathbf{x}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{x}_{n})^{1/2}.

Substituting into (
62
),

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{n}\left(\mathcal{B}^{(L)}_{1}\right)\leq\frac{\Gamma_{T}(\mathbf{x}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{x}_{n})^{1/2}}{n}\,\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}^{n}(0,1)}\left[\sqrt{S_{T}(\varepsilon)}\right].

By Jensen’s inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}^{n}(0,1)}\left[\sqrt{S_{T}(\varepsilon)}\right]\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}^{n}(0,1)}[S_{T}(\varepsilon)]}.

By the tree scan lemma (
\Cref
lem:tree-scan-rigorous),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}^{n}(0,1)}[S_{T}(\varepsilon)]\leq C_{0}\,n\ln(2n).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{n}(\mathcal{B}_{1})\leq C\,\frac{\sqrt{\ln(2n)}}{n}\left(\sum_{\{i,j\}\in E_{T}}\|\mathbf{x}_{i}-\mathbf{x}_{j}\|_{2}^{\alpha}+R_{X}^{\alpha}\right)^{1/2}.

(70)

By homogeneity of (
62
), multiplying the right-hand side by
r
yields

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{n}\left(\mathcal{B}^{(L)}_{r}\right)\leq C\,\frac{r\sqrt{\ln(2n)}}{n}\left(\sum_{\{i,j\}\in E_{T}}\|\mathbf{x}_{i}-\mathbf{x}_{j}\|_{2}^{\alpha}+R_{X}^{\alpha}\right)^{1/2}.

(71)

To derive the radius-only bound, note that
T
is a tree on
[n]
, hence
|E_{T}|=n-1
. Moreover, for every
\{i,j\}\in E_{T}
,

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{x}_{i}-\mathbf{x}_{j}\|_{2}\leq\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|_{2}+\|\mathbf{x}_{j}\|_{2}\leq 2R_{X}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\sum_{\left\{i,j\right\}\in E_{T}}\left\|\mathbf{x}_{i}-\mathbf{x}_{j}\right\|_{2}^{\alpha}+R_{X}^{\alpha}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}\left(n-1\right)\left(2R_{X}\right)^{\alpha}+R_{X}^{\alpha}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{=}}\left(\left(n-1\right)2^{\alpha}+1\right)R_{X}^{\alpha}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{\leq}}\left(2^{\alpha}n+1\right)R_{X}^{\alpha}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(d)}}{{\leq}}C^{\prime}nR_{X}^{\alpha}.

Here (a) uses
\left|E_{T}\right|=n-1
together with
\left\|\mathbf{x}_{i}-\mathbf{x}_{j}\right\|_{2}\leq 2R_{X}
,
(b) factors out
R_{X}^{\alpha}
,
(c) bounds
n-1\leq n
,
and (d) absorbs constants into a universal constant
C^{\prime}>0
. Substituting into (
71
), we obtain

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{n}\left(\mathcal{B}^{(L)}_{r}\right)\leq C\,\frac{r\sqrt{\ln(2n)}}{n}\left(C^{\prime}nR_{X}^{\alpha}\right)^{1/2}=C\sqrt{C^{\prime}}\,\frac{r\sqrt{\ln(2n)}}{\sqrt{n}}R_{X}^{\alpha/2}.

Since
\alpha/2=2^{-(L-2)}/2=2^{-(L-1)}
, after adjusting the universal constant, we conclude that

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{n}\left(\mathcal{B}^{(L)}_{r}\right)\leq C\,\frac{r\,R_{X}^{\,2^{-(L-1)}}\sqrt{\ln(2n)}}{\sqrt{n}},

(72)

Hence,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{X}^{n}}\left[R_{X}^{\,2^{-(L-1)}}\right]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{X}^{n}}\left[\left(R_{X}^{2}\right)^{2^{-L}}\right]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leq}}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{X}^{n}}\left[R_{X}^{2}\right]\right)^{2^{-L}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{=}}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{X}^{n}}\left[\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\left\|\mathbf{X}_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]\right)^{2^{-L}}.

Here (a) rewrites the exponent,
(b) applies Jensen’s inequality for concave functions (since
2^{-L}\in(0,1]
, the map
x\mapsto x^{2^{-L}}
is concave on
[0,\infty)
),
and (c) uses the definition of
R_{X}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{n}\left(\mathcal{B}^{(L)}_{r}\right)\leq C\,\frac{r\sqrt{\ln(2n)}}{\sqrt{n}}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{X}^{n}}\left[\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\left\|X_{i}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]\right)^{2^{-L}}.

which is (
28
).

B.7
Proof of
\Cref
thm:excess-risk-BKL

{noheadproof}

We proceed in two steps:

Step-1
(expected deviation via Gaussian complexity):
For a given training set
s\coloneq D_{XY,n}=\left\{\left(\mathbf{x}_{i},y_{i}\right)\right\}_{i=1}^{n}\in\left(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\right)^{n}
, define the empirical-process fluctuation

\displaystyle\Phi\left(s\right)\coloneqq\sup_{f\in\mathcal{B}^{(L)}_{r}}\left(\mathcal{R}_{n,s}\left(f\right)-\mathcal{R}\left(f\right)\right)+\sup_{f\in\mathcal{B}^{(L)}_{r}}\left(\mathcal{R}\left(f\right)-\mathcal{R}_{n,s}\left(f\right)\right),

where
\mathcal{R}_{n,s}\left(f\right)\coloneqq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell\left(y_{i},f\left(\mathbf{x}_{i}\right)\right)
. Let
S=\left\{\left(X_{i},Y_{i}\right)\right\}_{i=1}^{n}\sim\nu_{XY}^{n}
,
\bm{\sigma}=(\sigma_{i})_{i=1}^{n}\sim\mathrm{Rad}^{n}
and
\bm{\varepsilon}=(\varepsilon_{i})_{i=1}^{n}\sim\mathcal{N}^{n}(0,1)
. Then, for every
r>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{X}^{n}}\Phi\left(S\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(a\right)}}{{\leq}}4\,\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{Rad}^{n},\nu_{X}^{n}}\left[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{B}^{(L)}_{r}}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{i}\ell\left(Y_{i},f\left(X_{i}\right)\right)\right|\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(b\right)}}{{\leq}}4L_{\ell}\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{Rad}^{n},\nu_{X}^{n}}\left[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{B}^{(L)}_{r}}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{i}f\left(X_{i}\right)\right|\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(c\right)}}{{\leq}}4\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{2}}\,L_{\ell}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}^{n}(0,1),\nu_{X}^{n}}\left[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{B}^{(L)}_{r}}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{i}f\left(X_{i}\right)\right|\right]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(d\right)}}{{=}}4\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{2}}\,L_{\ell}\mathcal{G}_{n}\left(\mathcal{B}^{(L)}_{r}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(e\right)}}{{\leq}}4\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{2}}\,L_{\ell}\frac{Cr\ln(2n)\sqrt[4]{B}}{\sqrt{n}}=\frac{4\sqrt{\pi}L_{\ell}Cr\ln(2n)\sqrt[4]{B}}{\sqrt{2n}}\eqcolon U_{r}.

(73)

Here (a) follows from symmetrization
(
28
, Lemma
26.2
)
, (b) applies the contraction inequality
(
23
, Eq. (4.20))
, (c) uses the Gaussian–Rademacher comparison bound
(
30
, Exercise (5.5))
, (d) invokes the definition of Gaussian complexity, and (e) applies Theorem
8
. Now fix an index
i\in[n]
and form
s^{(i)}
by replacing the
i
-th sample

\left(\mathbf{x}_{i},y_{i}\right)
with an arbitrary
\left(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\prime},y_{i}^{\prime}\right)\in\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}
. For any
f,\tilde{f}\in\mathcal{B}^{(L)}_{r}
,

\displaystyle\left|\Phi\left(s\right)-\Phi\left(s^{(i)}\right)\right|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(a\right)}}{{=}}\left|\sup_{f,\tilde{f}\in\mathcal{B}^{(L)}_{r}}\left[\left(\mathcal{R}\left(f\right)-\mathcal{R}_{n,s}\left(f\right)\right)-\left(\mathcal{R}\left(\tilde{f}\right)-\mathcal{R}_{n,s}\left(\tilde{f}\right)\right)\right]\right.

\displaystyle\left.\qquad-\sup_{f,\tilde{f}\in\mathcal{B}^{(L)}_{r}}\left[\left(\mathcal{R}\left(f\right)-\mathcal{R}_{n,s^{(i)}}\left(f\right)\right)-\left(\mathcal{R}\left(\tilde{f}\right)-\mathcal{R}_{n,s^{(i)}}\left(\tilde{f}\right)\right)\right]\right|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(b\right)}}{{\leq}}\sup_{f,\tilde{f}\in\mathcal{B}^{(L)}_{r}}\left|\left[\left(\mathcal{R}\left(f\right)-\mathcal{R}_{n,s}\left(f\right)\right)-\left(\mathcal{R}\left(\tilde{f}\right)-\mathcal{R}_{n,s}\left(\tilde{f}\right)\right)\right]\right.

\displaystyle\left.\qquad\qquad-\left[\left(\mathcal{R}\left(f\right)-\mathcal{R}_{n,s^{(i)}}\left(f\right)\right)-\left(\mathcal{R}\left(\tilde{f}\right)-\mathcal{R}_{n,s^{(i)}}\left(\tilde{f}\right)\right)\right]\right|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(c\right)}}{{=}}\sup_{f,\tilde{f}\in\mathcal{B}^{(L)}_{r}}\frac{1}{n}\left|\left(\ell\left(y_{i},f\left(\mathbf{x}_{i}\right)\right)-\ell\left(y_{i},\tilde{f}\left(\mathbf{x}_{i}\right)\right)\right)-\left(\ell\left(y_{i}^{\prime},f\left(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\prime}\right)\right)-\ell\left(y_{i}^{\prime},\tilde{f}\left(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\prime}\right)\right)\right)\right|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(d\right)}}{{\leq}}\sup_{f,\tilde{f}\in\mathcal{B}^{(L)}_{r}}\frac{1}{n}\left|\ell\left(y_{i},f\left(\mathbf{x}_{i}\right)\right)-\ell\left(y_{i},\tilde{f}\left(\mathbf{x}_{i}\right)\right)\right|+\sup_{f,\tilde{f}\in\mathcal{B}^{(L)}_{r}}\frac{1}{n}\left|\ell\left(y_{i}^{\prime},f\left(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\prime}\right)\right)-\ell\left(y_{i}^{\prime},\tilde{f}\left(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\prime}\right)\right)\right|.

(74)

Here (a) uses the definition of
\Phi
together with
\Cref
lem:sup-inf-pairwise, (b) applies the inequality

\displaystyle\left|\sup_{u}A(u)-\sup_{u}B(u)\right|\leq\sup_{u}\left|A(u)-B(u)\right|,

where
u=\left(f,\tilde{f}\right)
and

\displaystyle A(u)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathcal{R}\left(f\right)-\mathcal{R}_{n,s}\left(f\right)-\left(\mathcal{R}\left(\tilde{f}\right)-\mathcal{R}_{n,s}\left(\tilde{f}\right)\right),

\displaystyle B(u)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathcal{R}\left(f\right)-\mathcal{R}_{n,s^{(i)}}\left(f\right)-\left(\mathcal{R}\left(\tilde{f}\right)-\mathcal{R}_{n,s^{(i)}}\left(\tilde{f}\right)\right).

(c) uses that
\mathcal{R}\left(\cdot\right)
is independent of the sample, while
\mathcal{R}_{n,s}
and
\mathcal{R}_{n,s^{(i)}}
differ only in the
i
-th summand, yielding the factor
1/n
. Finally,(d) applies the triangle inequality. Since
\ell
is
L_{\ell}
-Lipschitz in its second argument,
for all
y\in\mathcal{Y}
and all
\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X}
,

\displaystyle\left|\ell\left(y,f\left(\mathbf{x}\right)\right)-\ell\left(y,\tilde{f}\left(\mathbf{x}\right)\right)\right|\leq L_{\ell}\left|f\left(\mathbf{x}\right)-\tilde{f}\left(\mathbf{x}\right)\right|.

Moreover, for all
f,\tilde{f}\in\mathcal{B}^{(L)}_{r}
and all
\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X}
,

\displaystyle\left|f\left(\mathbf{x}\right)-\tilde{f}\left(\mathbf{x}\right)\right|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(a\right)}}{{=}}\left|\left(f-\tilde{f}\right)\left(\mathbf{x}\right)\right|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(b\right)}}{{\leq}}\left\|f-\tilde{f}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}_{a}}^{(L)}}\left\|\mathbf{x}\right\|_{2}^{1/2^{L-1}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(c\right)}}{{\leq}}\sqrt{2}\left(\left\|f\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}_{b}}^{(L)}}+\left\|\tilde{f}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}_{c}}^{(L)}}\right)\left\|\mathbf{x}\right\|_{2}^{1/2^{L-1}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(d\right)}}{{\leq}}2\sqrt{2}r\,B^{1/2^{L}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\left(e\right)}}{{\leq}}2\sqrt{2}r\,\sqrt[4]{B}.

(75)

Here (a) is algebraic.
To apply the RKHS pointwise bound, we embed
f
and
\tilde{f}
into a common ladder RKHS.
Indeed, if
f
and
\tilde{f}
are realized by ladders
\mathcal{H}^{(1:L)}_{\hat{\mu}_{b}}
and
\mathcal{H}^{(1:L)}_{\hat{\mu}_{c}}
, respectively, the mixture construction in the Step-1 of the proof of
\Cref
thm:charachteriz_funspace
2
yields a ladder
\mathcal{H}^{(1:L)}_{\hat{\mu}_{a}}
such that
f-\tilde{f}\in\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}_{a}}^{(L)}
. (b) applies the pointwise bound (
17
) from
\Cref
thm:charachteriz_funspace
1
. (c) uses
\Cref
lem:measures_inequality. Finally, (d) exploits
f,\tilde{f}\in\mathcal{B}^{(L)}_{r}
together with
\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X}}\left\|\mathbf{x}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq B
and (e) uses
L\geq 2
. Combining (
B.7
) and (
B.7
) yields

\displaystyle\sup_{s,s^{(i)}}\left|\Phi\left(s\right)-\Phi\left(s^{(i)}\right)\right|\leq\frac{4\sqrt{2}L_{\ell}r\sqrt[4]{B}}{n}\eqqcolon c.

(76)

Thus
\Phi(S)
satisfies the bounded-differences condition with constants

c_{i}=c
for all
i\in[n]
. Applying McDiarmid’s inequality
(see, e.g.,
25
), which states that

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\Phi(S)-\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{X}^{n}}\Phi(S)\geq t\right)\leq e^{-\frac{2t^{2}}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}c_{i}^{2}}},\qquad\text{for any }t>0,

and using
c_{i}=c
yields
\sum_{i=1}^{n}c_{i}^{2}=nc^{2}
. Setting

\displaystyle t:=c\sqrt{\frac{n}{2}\ln\left(\frac{1}{\delta}\right)}

gives that with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\Phi\left(S\right)\leq\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{X}^{n}}\Phi\left(S\right)+4\sqrt{2}L_{\ell}r\sqrt[4]{B}\sqrt{\frac{\ln\left(1/\delta\right)}{n}}.

(77)

Define

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sup_{f\in\mathcal{B}^{(L)}_{r}}\left(\mathcal{R}_{n}\left(f\right)-\mathcal{R}\left(f\right)\right),

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sup_{f\in\mathcal{B}^{(L)}_{r}}\left(\mathcal{R}\left(f\right)-\mathcal{R}_{n}\left(f\right)\right).

Then
\Phi\left(S\right)=A+B
. For any
f\in\mathcal{B}^{(L)}_{r}
, one has

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{R}\left(f\right)-\mathcal{R}_{n}\left(f\right)\right|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}\max\left\{\mathcal{R}\left(f\right)-\mathcal{R}_{n}\left(f\right),\,\mathcal{R}_{n}\left(f\right)-\mathcal{R}\left(f\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leq}}\max\left\{A,B\right\}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{\leq}}A+B\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(d)}}{{=}}\Phi\left(S\right).

Here (a) is the identity
|u|=\max\{u,-u\}
, (b) follows from the definitions of
A
and
B
, (c) is elementary, and (d) uses
\Phi(S)=A+B
. Taking the supremum over
f\in\mathcal{B}^{(L)}_{r}
yields

\displaystyle\sup_{f\in\mathcal{B}^{(L)}_{r}}\left|\mathcal{R}\left(f\right)-\mathcal{R}_{n}\left(f\right)\right|\leq\Phi\left(S\right).

(78)

combining (
77
) and (
78
) with the expectation bound (
B.7
) yields

\displaystyle\sup_{f\in\mathcal{B}^{(L)}_{r}}\left|\mathcal{R}\left(f\right)-\mathcal{R}_{n}\left(f\right)\right|\leq U_{r}+4\sqrt{2}L_{\ell}r\sqrt[4]{B}\sqrt{\frac{\ln\left(1/\delta\right)}{n}}=:\Psi_{n}\left(r,\delta,C\right).

(79)

Step-2
(wellspecified excess risk bound):
Let
f^{*}\in\arg\min_{f\in\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L)}}\mathcal{R}(f)
(if it exists), and define

\displaystyle r\coloneqq 2\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f^{*}).

We claim that, under the stated choice of
\lambda
, one has

\displaystyle\widehat{C}^{(L)}(\hat{f}_{\lambda})\leq r.

(80)

Indeed, by optimality of
\hat{f}_{\lambda}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{n}(\hat{f}_{\lambda})+\lambda\widehat{C}^{(L)}(\hat{f}_{\lambda})\leq\mathcal{R}_{n}(f^{*})+\lambda\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f^{*}).

Rearranging gives

\displaystyle\lambda\bigl(\widehat{C}^{(L)}(\hat{f}_{\lambda})-\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f^{*})\bigr)\leq\mathcal{R}_{n}(f^{*})-\mathcal{R}_{n}(\hat{f}_{\lambda}).

Using the uniform deviation bound (
79
) with radius
r_{0}:=\max\{\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f^{*}),\widehat{C}^{(L)}(\hat{f}_{\lambda})\}
yields

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{n}(f^{*})-\mathcal{R}_{n}(\hat{f}_{\lambda})\leq 2\,\Psi_{n}(r_{0},\delta,C).

Hence

\displaystyle\lambda\bigl(\widehat{C}^{(L)}(\hat{f}_{\lambda})-\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f^{*})\bigr)\leq 2\,\Psi_{n}(r_{0},\delta,C).

If
\widehat{C}^{(L)}(\hat{f}_{\lambda})>2\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f^{*})
, then
r_{0}=\widehat{C}^{(L)}(\hat{f}_{\lambda})
and

\displaystyle\widehat{C}^{(L)}(\hat{f}_{\lambda})-\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f^{*})\geq\frac{1}{2}\widehat{C}^{(L)}(\hat{f}_{\lambda})=\frac{r_{0}}{2},

which implies

\displaystyle\lambda\leq\frac{4\,\Psi_{n}(r_{0},\delta,C)}{r_{0}}.

By the explicit form of
\Psi_{n}
, this contradicts the assumed choice of
\lambda
.
Therefore (
80
) holds. Using the standard decomposition,

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}(\hat{f}_{\lambda})-\mathcal{R}(f^{*})

\displaystyle=\bigl[\mathcal{R}(\hat{f}_{\lambda})-\mathcal{R}_{n}(\hat{f}_{\lambda})\bigr]+\bigl[\mathcal{R}_{n}(\hat{f}_{\lambda})-\mathcal{R}_{n}(f^{*})\bigr]+\bigl[\mathcal{R}_{n}(f^{*})-\mathcal{R}(f^{*})\bigr].

By optimality of
\hat{f}_{\lambda}
, the middle term is non-positive, hence

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}(\hat{f}_{\lambda})-\mathcal{R}(f^{*})\leq 2\sup_{f\in\mathcal{B}^{(L)}_{r}}\left|\mathcal{R}(f)-\mathcal{R}_{n}(f)\right|.

Applying (
79
) with
r=2\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f^{*})
gives

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(\hat{f}_{\lambda})\leq 2\,\Psi_{n}\left(2\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f^{*}),\delta,C\right).

Substituting the expression of
\Psi_{n}
and simplifying constants yields

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(\hat{f}_{\lambda})\leq\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f^{*})\frac{8\pi L_{\ell}\ln(2n)\sqrt[4]{B}}{\sqrt{n}}+16\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f^{*})L_{\ell}\sqrt[4]{B}\sqrt{\frac{\ln(1/\delta)}{n}},

which proves (
33
).

In this section, we present our own lemmas used in
\Cref
app.

Lemma 12

(pointwise evaluation, Lipschitz continuity)
.

Let
\mathcal{H}_{k}
be an RKHS on
\mathcal{X}
with kernel function
k
. Then, for each
f\in\mathcal{H}_{k}

\displaystyle|f(\mathbf{x})|\leq\|f\|_{\mathcal{H}_{k}}[k(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x})]^{1/2},\quad\forall\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X},

(81)

\displaystyle|f(\mathbf{x})-f(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})|\leq\|f\|_{\mathcal{H}_{k}}d_{k}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime}),\quad\forall\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X},

(82)

where

\displaystyle d_{k}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left[k(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x})-2k(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime})+k(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\right]^{1/2}.

(83)

Proof.

•

Part-1 (pointwise evaluation)
: (
81
) is a known result
(
29
, Lemma 4.23)
.

•

Part-2 (Lipschitz continuity)
: One has

\displaystyle\left|f(\mathbf{x})-f(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\right|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}\left|\langle f,k(\mathbf{x},\cdot)-k(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\cdot)\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{k}}\right|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leq}}\|f\|_{\mathcal{H}_{k}}\|k(\mathbf{x},\cdot)-k(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\cdot)\|_{\mathcal{H}_{k}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{=}}\|f\|_{\mathcal{H}_{k}}\underbrace{\sqrt{k(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x})-2k(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime})+k(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\mathbf{x}^{\prime})}}_{=d_{k}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime})}.

(a) follows from the reproducing property and the linearity of the inner product, (b) is implied by the CBS inequality, (c) comes from the fact that in a Hilbert space the norm is induced by the inner product, the linearity of the inner product, the reproducing property, the fact that
k
is a symmetric function, and the definition of
d_{k}
.

∎

Lemma 13

(nested pointwise evaluation and Lipschitz continuity)
.

Let
L\geq 2
and
\mathcal{X}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
compact. Suppose that
\mathcal{S}^{(1:L-1)}_{\mu}
and
\mu^{(1:L-1)}
(in
\Cref
def:BKLdef) satisfy Assumption
3.1
; let the corresponding canonical sets and measures (according to
\Cref
rem:BKL_def) be
\mathcal{S}^{(1:L-1)}_{\hat{\mu}}\coloneq\left(S_{\hat{\mu}}^{(l)}\right)_{l=1}^{L-1}\coloneq\left(\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(l)}\right)\right)_{l=1}^{L-1}
and
\hat{\mu}^{(1:L-1)}\coloneq\left(\hat{\mu}^{(l)}\right)_{l=1}^{L-1}\in\times_{l=1}^{L-1}\mathcal{P}_{1,1}\left(\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(l)}\right)\right)
, respectively. Let

\displaystyle\hat{k}^{(l)}

\displaystyle=k\left[\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(l)}\right),\hat{\mu}^{(l)}\right],

\displaystyle d_{\hat{k}^{(l)}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\coloneq\left[\hat{k}^{(l)}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x})-2\hat{k}^{(l)}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime})+\hat{k}^{(l)}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\right]^{1/2},

with
l\in[L-1]
and
\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}
. Let
k^{(0)}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime})=\mathbf{x}^{\top}\mathbf{x}^{\prime}
. Then,

\displaystyle\left[\hat{k}^{(l)}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x})\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt[2^{l}]{\|\mathbf{x}\|_{2}},\quad\forall\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X},

(84)

\displaystyle d_{\hat{k}^{(l)}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt[2^{l}]{\|\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\|_{2}},\quad\forall\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X},

(85)

where
l\in\{0,1,\ldots,L-1\}
.

Proof.

We prove the statement by induction on
l
. Let
\mathcal{H}^{(1:L)}_{\hat{\mu}}\coloneq\left(\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(l)}\right)_{l=1}^{L}
with
\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(l+1)}\coloneq\mathcal{H}_{\hat{k}^{(l)}}
for
l\in[L-1]
and
\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(1)}\coloneq\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(1)}
.

•

l=0
: In this case, one has

\displaystyle k^{(0)}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=\|\mathbf{x}\|_{2}^{2},\quad\forall\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X},

\displaystyle d_{k^{(0)}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\|\mathbf{x}\|_{2}^{2}-2\mathbf{x}^{\top}\mathbf{x}^{\prime}+\|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{2}}=\|\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\|_{2},\quad\text{for all}\quad\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X},

thus (
84
), and (
85
) hold.

•

Induction hypothesis (
l
): We assume that (
84
) and (
85
) hold for
l
.

•

Induction step (
l\to l+1
):

–

Part-1
(
\left[\hat{k}^{(l)}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x})\right]^{1/2}
):
For all
\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X}
, one has

\displaystyle\hat{k}^{(l+1)}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}\int_{\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(l+1)}\right)}k_{u}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x})\mathrm{d}\hat{\mu}^{(l+1)}(u)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{=}}\int_{\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(l+1)}\right)}|u(\mathbf{x})|\mathrm{d}\hat{\mu}^{(l+1)}(u)

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{\leq}}\int_{\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(l+1)}\right)}\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(l+1)}}\left(\hat{k}^{(l)}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x})\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}\hat{\mu}^{(l+1)}(u)

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(d)}}{{=}}\left[\int_{\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(l+1)}\right)}\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(l+1)}}\mathrm{d}\hat{\mu}^{(l+1)}(u)\right]\left[\hat{k}^{(l)}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x})\right]^{1/2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(e)}}{{=}}\left\|\hat{\mu}^{(l+1)}\right\|_{\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(l+1)}\right),1}\left[\hat{k}^{(l)}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x})\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(f)}}{{\leq}}\left\|\hat{\mu}^{(l+1)}\right\|_{\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(l+1)}\right),1}\sqrt[2^{l}]{\|\mathbf{x}\|_{2}}\prod_{j=1}^{l}\sqrt[2^{l+1-j}]{\|\hat{\mu}^{(j)}\|_{\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(j)}\right),1}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(g)}}{{=}}\sqrt[2^{l}]{\|\mathbf{x}\|_{2}}.

(a) follows from
\hat{k}^{(l+1)}=k\left[\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(l+1)}\right),\hat{\mu}^{(l+1)}\right]
, (b) is implied by (
86
) in
\Cref
lem:BK, (c) comes from (
81
) in
\Cref
lem:RKHSfunctions_regularity
and the monotonicity of integration, (d) follows from the linearity of the integration, the definition of
\|\cdot\|_{\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(l+1)}\right),1}
gives (e), the induction hypothesis was used in (f), (g) is implied by using
\|\hat{\mu}^{(j)}\|_{\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(j)}\right),1}=1
for
j\in[l]
, and merging terms. Hence, the 1st part of the statement holds for
l+1
.

–

Part-2
[
d_{\hat{k}^{(l)}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime})
]:
For all
\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}
, one has

\displaystyle\left[d_{\hat{k}^{(l+1)}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\right]^{2}=

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}\hat{k}^{(l+1)}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x})-2\hat{k}^{(l+1)}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime})+\hat{k}^{(l+1)}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\mathbf{x}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{=}}\int_{\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(l+1)}\right)}k_{u}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x})\mathrm{d}\hat{\mu}^{(l+1)}(u)+\int_{\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(l+1)}\right)}k_{u}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\mathrm{d}\hat{\mu}^{(l+1)}(u)

\displaystyle\quad-2\int_{\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(l+1)}\right)}k_{u}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\mathrm{d}\hat{\mu}^{(l+1)}(u)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{=}}\int_{\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(l+1)}\right)}|u(\mathbf{x})|\mathrm{d}\hat{\mu}^{(l+1)}(u)+\int_{\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(l+1)}\right)}|u(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})|\mathrm{d}\hat{\mu}^{(l+1)}(u)

\displaystyle\quad-2\int_{\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(l+1)}\right)}\frac{|u(\mathbf{x})|+|u(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})|-|u(\mathbf{x})-u(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})|}{2}\mathrm{d}\hat{\mu}^{(l+1)}(u)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(d)}}{{=}}\int_{\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(l+1)}\right)}|u(\mathbf{x})-u(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})|\mathrm{d}\hat{\mu}^{(l+1)}(u)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(e)}}{{\leq}}\int_{\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(l+1)}\right)}\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(l+1)}}\left[d_{\hat{k}^{(l)}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\right]^{1/2}\mathrm{d}\hat{\mu}^{(l+1)}(u)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(f)}}{{=}}\left[\int_{\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(l+1)}\right)}\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(l+1)}}\mathrm{d}\hat{\mu}^{(l+1)}(u)\right]\left[d_{\hat{k}^{(l)}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(g)}}{{\leq}}\left\|\hat{\mu}^{(l+1)}\right\|_{\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(l+1)}\right),1}\sqrt[2^{l}]{\|\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\|_{2}}\prod_{j=1}^{l}\sqrt[2^{l+1-j}]{\|\hat{\mu}^{(j)}\|_{\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(j)}\right),1}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(h)}}{{=}}\sqrt[2^{l}]{\|\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\|_{2}}\prod_{j=1}^{l+1}\sqrt[2^{l+1-j}]{\|\hat{\mu}^{(j)}\|_{\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(j)}\right),1}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(j)}}{{=}}\sqrt[2^{l}]{\|\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\|_{2}}.

In (a) we applied the definition of
d_{\hat{k}^{(l+1)}}
[(
83
)], (b) comes from
\hat{k}^{(l+1)}=k\left[\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(l+1)}\right),\hat{\mu}^{(l+1)}\right]
, (c) follows from (
86
) in
\Cref
lem:BK and the definition of
k_{u}
, (d) follows from the linearity of integration and the simplification of terms, (e) holds by
\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\mu}}^{(l+1)}=\mathcal{H}_{\hat{k}^{(l)}}
and (
82
) in
\Cref
lem:RKHSfunctions_regularity, the linearity of the integration implies (f), in (g) the definition of
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(l+1)}\right),1}
and the induction hypothesis were applied, terms were rearranged in (h). Finally, (j) is implied by using
\|\hat{\mu}^{(j)}\|_{\mathbb{S}_{1}\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(j)}\right),1}=1
for
j\in[l+1]
. Hence the 2nd part of the statement holds for
l+1
.

∎

Lemma 14

(verification of (
9
) for BKL)
.

Assume that
\mathcal{X}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
is compact. Let
\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(1)}
be the RKHS of the linear functionals

\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X}\mapsto\bm{\omega}^{\top}\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}
(
\bm{\omega}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
). For
l\geq 1
, consider a measurable subset
\mathcal{S}^{(l)}_{\mu}\subseteq\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(l)}
of the already defined RKHS
\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(l)}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{\mathcal{X}}
, and let
\mu^{(l)}\in\mathcal{P}_{1}\left(\mathcal{S}^{(l)}_{\mu}\right)
. For
u\in\mathcal{S}^{(l)}_{\mu}
, let
k_{u}
defined according to (
8
). Then, for all
\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X}
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{S}^{(l)}_{\mu}}k_{u}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x})\mathrm{d}\mu^{(l)}(u)<\infty,

hence the kernel
k^{(l)}=k\left[\mathcal{S}^{(l)}_{\mu},\mu^{(l)}\right]
and the associated
RKHS
\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(l+1)}\coloneqq\mathcal{H}_{k^{(l)}}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{\mathcal{X}}
are well-defined.

Proof.

Our goal is to show that
\int_{\mathcal{S}_{\mu}^{(l)}}k_{u}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x})\mathrm{d}\mu^{(l)}(u)<\infty
for all
l\in\mathbb{Z}^{>0}
and
\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X}
. Recalling that
k_{u}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x})=k^{(\mathrm{B})}(u(\mathbf{x}),u(\mathbf{x}))
, where
k^{(\mathrm{B})}
is the Brownian kernel, our target quantity takes the form

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{S}_{\mu}^{(l)}}k_{u}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x})\mathrm{d}\mu^{(l)}(u)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{S}_{\mu}^{(l)}}\frac{|u(\mathbf{x})|+|u(\mathbf{x})|-|u(\mathbf{x})-u(\mathbf{x})|}{2}\mathrm{d}\mu^{(l)}(u)=\int_{\mathcal{S}_{\mu}^{(l)}}|u(\mathbf{x})|\mathrm{d}\mu^{(l)}(u);

(86)

this is the form of our target we study in the sequel. We proceed by induction.

•

l=1
:
In this case, with any
u=u_{\bm{\omega}}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(1)}=\left\{\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X}\mapsto\bm{\omega}^{\top}\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}\,:\mathbf{\bm{\omega}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\right\}
, we have for an arbitrary
\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X}
that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{S}_{\mu}^{(1)}}|u(\mathbf{x})|\mathrm{d}\mu^{(1)}(u)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}\int_{\mathcal{S}_{\mu}^{(1)}}\left\|\bm{\omega}\right\|_{2}\left\|\mathbf{x}\right\|_{2}\mathrm{d}\mu^{(1)}(\bm{\omega})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leq}}\int_{\mathcal{S}_{\mu}^{(1)}}\left\|\bm{\omega}\right\|_{2}C\mathrm{d}\mu^{(1)}(\bm{\omega})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{=}}C\int_{\mathcal{S}_{\mu}^{(1)}}\left\|\bm{\omega}\right\|_{2}\mathrm{d}\mu^{(1)}(\bm{\omega})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(d)}}{{<}}\infty.

In (a) we applied (
81
) from
\Cref
lem:RKHSfunctions_regularity and the monotonicity of integration, in (b) we used the fact that
\mathcal{X}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
is compact, thus bounded, hence there exists
C\in\mathbb{R}^{\geq 0}
such that
\left\|\mathbf{x}\right\|_{2}\leq C
for all
\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X}
, with the monotonicity of integration, (c) comes from the linearity of integration. (d) follows from the assumption that
\mu^{(1)}\in\mathcal{P}_{1}\left(\mathcal{S}_{\mu}^{(1)}\right)
.
This ensures that the kernel
k^{(1)}=k\left[\mathcal{S}_{\mu}^{(1)},\mu^{(1)}\right]
and the associated RKHS
\mathcal{H}^{(2)}\coloneq\mathcal{H}_{k^{(1)}}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{\mathcal{X}}
are well-defined.

•

Induction hypothesis (
l
): We assume that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{S}_{\mu}^{(l)}}|u(\mathbf{x})|\mathrm{d}\mu^{(l)}(u)<\infty\quad\text{ for all }\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X}

and hence the kernel
k^{(l)}=k\left[\mathcal{S}_{\mu}^{(l)},\mu^{(l)}\right]
and the associated RKHS
\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(l+1)}\coloneq\mathcal{H}_{k^{(l)}}
are well-defined.

•

Induction step (
l\rightarrow l+1
): With arbitrary
\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X}
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{S}_{\mu}^{(l+1)}}|u(\mathbf{x})|\mathrm{d}\mu^{(l+1)}(u)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}\int_{\mathcal{S}_{\mu}^{(l+1)}}\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(l+1)}}\sqrt{k^{(l)}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x})}\mathrm{d}\mu^{(l+1)}(u)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{=}}\sqrt{k^{(l)}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x})}\int_{\mathcal{S}_{\mu}^{(l+1)}}\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(l+1)}}\mathrm{d}\mu^{(l+1)}(u)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{<}}\infty.

(a) is implied by (
81
) from
\Cref
lem:RKHSfunctions_regularity and the monotonicity of integration, (b) follows from the linearity of integration, (c) is implied by the finiteness of
k^{(l)}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x})
(coming from the induction hypothesis)
and the assumption that
\mu^{(l+1)}\in\mathcal{P}_{1}\left(\mathcal{S}_{\mu}^{(l+1)}\right)
. Hence, the kernel
k^{(l+1)}=k\left[\mathcal{S}_{\mu}^{(l+1)},\mu^{(l+1)}\right]
and the associated RKHS
\mathcal{H}^{(l+2)}\coloneq\mathcal{H}_{k^{(l+1)}}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{\mathcal{X}}
are well-defined.

∎

Condition (
9
) leads to the following result on representation.

Lemma 15

(representation of the elements of
\mathcal{H}^{(1:L)}_{\mu}
)
.

Let
f^{(1:L)}\in\mathcal{H}^{(1:L)}_{\mu}=\left(\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(l)}\right)_{l=1}^{L}
, where
\mathcal{H}^{(1:L)}_{\mu}
is defined according to
\Cref
def:BKLdef with
\mathcal{S}_{\mu}^{(l)}\subseteq\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(l)}
and
\mu^{(l)}\in\mathcal{P}_{1}\left(\mathcal{S}^{(l)}_{\mu}\right)
for all
l\in[L-1]
. Then for each
l\in[L-1]
, the elements of
\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(l+1)}
take the form

\displaystyle f^{(l+1)}(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{S}_{\mu}^{(l)}}g_{u}^{(l)}\left(u(\mathbf{x})\right)\mathrm{d}\mu^{(l)}(u),\quad\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X},

where
g_{u}^{(l)}\in\mathcal{H}_{k^{(\mathrm{B})}}
for all
u\in\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(l)}
, with norm

\displaystyle\left\|f^{(l+1)}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\mu}^{(l+1)}}^{2}=

\displaystyle\hskip-21.33955pt=\inf\left\{\int_{\mathcal{S}_{\mu}^{(l)}}\left\|b_{u}^{(l)}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{k^{(\mathrm{B})}}}^{2}\mathrm{d}\mu^{(l)}(u)\,:\,f^{(l+1)}(\mathbf{x})=\int_{\mathcal{S}_{\mu}^{(l)}}b_{u}^{(l)}(u(\mathbf{x}))\mathrm{d}\mu^{(l)}(u),\,\forall\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X},b^{(l)}\in\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\oplus}^{(l)}\right\}

(87)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\oplus}^{(l)}
