where (a) follows from
A_{t}^{+}\left(\mathbf{a}\right)=\left\{i\in[n]:a_{i}\geq t\right\}
, and (b) is a direct reformulation. Similarly,

\displaystyle A_{t}^{+}\left(\mathbf{a}\right)=[n]\Longleftrightarrow a_{i}\geq t\ \forall i\in[n]\Longleftrightarrow t\leq\min_{i\in[n]}a_{i}.

Hence, for
t\geq 0
, the condition
\partial_{T}\left(A_{t}^{+}\left(\mathbf{a}\right)\right)=0
implies

\displaystyle\left\{\partial_{T}\left(A_{t}^{+}\left(\mathbf{a}\right)\right)=0\right\}\subseteq\left\{t>\max_{i}a_{i}\right\}\cup\left\{0\leq t\leq\min_{i}a_{i}\right\}.

(111)

Therefore, since both intervals in (
111
) have total length bounded by
\max_{i}\left|a_{i}\right|
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathds{1}_{\left\{\partial_{T}\left(A_{t}^{+}\left(\mathbf{a}\right)\right)=0\right\}}\mathrm{d}t\leq\max_{i\in[n]}\left|a_{i}\right|,

(112)

The same argument applied to
-\mathbf{a}
yields

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathds{1}_{\left\{\partial_{T}\left(A_{t}^{-}\left(\mathbf{a}\right)\right)=0\right\}}\mathrm{d}t\leq\max_{i\in[n]}\left|a_{i}\right|.

(113)

Combining (
109
), (
110
), (
112
), and (
113
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\left(\partial_{T}\left(A_{t}^{+}\left(\mathbf{a}\right)\right)\vee 1\right)+\left(\partial_{T}\left(A_{t}^{-}\left(\mathbf{a}\right)\right)\vee 1\right)\right)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}\sum_{\left\{i,j\right\}\in E_{T}}\left|a_{i}-a_{j}\right|+\max_{i\in[n]}\left|a_{i}\right|+\max_{i\in[n]}\left|a_{i}\right|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{=}}\sum_{\left\{i,j\right\}\in E_{T}}\left|a_{i}-a_{j}\right|+2\max_{i\in[n]}\left|a_{i}\right|,

where (a) uses the bounds established above, and (b) is a direct simplification. This proves (
106
).
∎

Lemma 29

(sup–inf representation via pairwise differences)
.

Let
F
be a nonempty set and let
A:F\to\mathbb{R}
be such that

\displaystyle\sup_{f\in F}A\left(f\right)<\infty,\qquad\inf_{f\in F}A\left(f\right)>-\infty.

Then

\displaystyle\sup_{f\in F}A\left(f\right)-\inf_{f\in F}A\left(f\right)=\sup_{f,\tilde{f}\in F}\left(A\left(f\right)-A\left(\tilde{f}\right)\right).

Proof.

Define

\displaystyle M\coloneqq\sup_{f\in F}A\left(f\right),\qquad m\coloneqq\inf_{f\in F}A\left(f\right).

Upper bound.

For any
f,\tilde{f}\in F
,

\displaystyle A\left(f\right)-A\left(\tilde{f}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}M-m,

where (a) uses
A(f)\leq M
and
A(\tilde{f})\geq m
. Taking the supremum yields

\displaystyle\sup_{f,\tilde{f}\in F}\left(A\left(f\right)-A\left(\tilde{f}\right)\right)\leq M-m.

(114)

Lower bound.

Let
\varepsilon>0
. By definition of supremum and infimum, there exist

f_{\varepsilon},\tilde{f}_{\varepsilon}\in F
such that

\displaystyle A\left(f_{\varepsilon}\right)>M-\varepsilon,\qquad A\left(\tilde{f}_{\varepsilon}\right)<m+\varepsilon.

Hence

\displaystyle A\left(f_{\varepsilon}\right)-A\left(\tilde{f}_{\varepsilon}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{>}}M-m-2\varepsilon,

where (a) follows by subtraction. Taking the supremum and letting
\varepsilon\downarrow 0
gives

\displaystyle\sup_{f,\tilde{f}\in F}\left(A\left(f\right)-A\left(\tilde{f}\right)\right)\geq M-m.

(115)

Combining (
114
) and (
115
) proves the claim.
∎

Lemma 30

(lower semicontinuity of
\widehat{C}^{(L)}
)
.

Let
\mathcal{X}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
be compact and assume that, for every
r>0
,
the set
\mathcal{B}^{(L)}_{r}
(defined in (
18
)) is closed in

\left(\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X}),\|\cdot\|_{\infty}\right)
.
Let
(f_{m})_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L)}
be a sequence such that

\displaystyle f_{m}\to f\qquad\text{in }\left(\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X}),\|\cdot\|_{\infty}\right)

for some
f\in\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X})
. If

\displaystyle\liminf_{m\to\infty}\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f_{m})<\infty,

then
f\in\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L)}
and

\displaystyle\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f)\leq\liminf_{m\to\infty}\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f_{m}).

Proof.

Set

\displaystyle a\coloneqq\liminf_{m\to\infty}\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f_{m}).

If
a=\infty
, there is nothing to prove. Hence assume that
a<\infty
. Let
\varepsilon>0
. By the definition of the lower limit, there exists a
subsequence
(f_{m_{k}})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
such that

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f_{m_{k}})=a.

Therefore, there exists
k_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
such that for all
k\geq k_{0}
,

\displaystyle\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f_{m_{k}})\leq a+\varepsilon.

Equivalently,

\displaystyle f_{m_{k}}\in\mathcal{B}^{(L)}_{a+\varepsilon},\qquad k\geq k_{0}.

Since
f_{m}\to f
uniformly on
\mathcal{X}
, the subsequence also satisfies

\displaystyle f_{m_{k}}\to f\qquad\text{in }\left(\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X}),\|\cdot\|_{\infty}\right).

By the closedness assumption,
\mathcal{B}^{(L)}_{a+\varepsilon}
is closed in

\left(\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X}),\|\cdot\|_{\infty}\right)
, hence

\displaystyle f\in\mathcal{B}^{(L)}_{a+\varepsilon}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle f\in\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L)},\qquad\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f)\leq a+\varepsilon.

Since
\varepsilon>0
was arbitrary, letting
\varepsilon\downarrow 0
yields

\displaystyle\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f)\leq a=\liminf_{m\to\infty}\widehat{C}^{(L)}(f_{m}).

∎

Lemma 31

(relatively compactness of quasi-norm balls in
\left(\widehat{\mathcal{H}}^{(L)},\widehat{C}^{(L)}\right)
)
.

Let
\mathcal{X}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
be compact and
r>0
. Let
\mathcal{B}^{(L)}_{r}
be defined in (
18
). Then
\mathcal{B}^{(L)}_{r}
is relatively compact in
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X})
with respect to
\|\cdot\|_{\infty}
.

Proof.

By (
16
) of
\Cref
thm:charachteriz_funspace
1
, for all
f\in\mathcal{B}^{(L)}_{r}
,

\displaystyle|f(\mathbf{x})-f(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})|\leq r\,\|\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\|_{2}^{1/2^{L-1}}\qquad\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}.

Thus
\mathcal{B}^{(L)}_{r}
is equicontinuous. Fix
\mathbf{x}_{0}\in\mathcal{X}
. Again by (
17
) of
\Cref
thm:charachteriz_funspace
1
,

\displaystyle|f(\mathbf{x}_{0})|\leq r\,\|\mathbf{x}_{0}\|_{2}^{1/2^{L-1}}.

Hence for all
\mathbf{x}
,

\displaystyle|f(\mathbf{x})|\leq|f(\mathbf{x}_{0})|+|f(\mathbf{x})-f(\mathbf{x}_{0})|\leq C_{r},

for a constant
C_{r}>0
independent of
f
. Since
\mathcal{X}
is compact and
\mathcal{B}^{(L)}_{r}
is equicontinuous and uniformly bounded, the Arzelà–Ascoli theorem (
\Cref
thm:arzela-ascoli) implies that
\mathcal{B}^{(L)}_{r}
is relatively compact in
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X})
.
∎

Lemma 32

(boundedness and Lipschitz constants of common loss functions)
.

Let
M>0
, and assume that
u,y\in[-M,M]
. Let
\ell:\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{Y}\to\mathbb{R}^{\geq 0}
, and assume that
\ell(\cdot,y)
is convex for any
y\in\mathcal{Y}
. Let
M_{\ell}=\sup_{u,y\in[-M,M]}\ell(u,y)
and
L_{\ell}=\sup_{u,y\in[-M,M]}\left|\partial_{u}\ell(u,y)\right|
(which satisfy the requirement in
\Cref
thm:excess-risk-BKL).
Then the followings hold.

1.

Squared loss:
For
\ell(u,y)\coloneq(u-y)^{2}
, one has
M_{\ell}=4M^{2}
and
L_{\ell}=4M
.

2.

Absolute loss:
For
\displaystyle\ell(u,y)=|u-y|
, one has
M_{\ell}=2M
and
L_{\ell}=1
.

3.

Huber loss
(parameter
\delta>0
): For

\displaystyle\ell(u,y;\delta)=\begin{cases}\tfrac{1}{2}(u-y)^{2},&\text{ if }|u-y|\leq\delta,\\[4.0pt]
\delta|u-y|-\tfrac{1}{2}\delta^{2},&\text{ if }|u-y|>\delta,\end{cases}

one has
M_{\ell}=\begin{cases}2M^{2},&\text{ if }\delta\geq 2M,\\[4.0pt]
2\delta M-\tfrac{1}{2}\delta^{2},&\text{ if }\delta<2M,\end{cases}
and
L_{\ell}=\delta
.

4.

\varepsilon
-insensitive loss
(
\varepsilon>0
): For

\displaystyle\ell(u,y;\varepsilon)=\max\{0,|u-y|-\varepsilon\}=\begin{cases}0,&\text{ if }|u-y|\leq\varepsilon,\\[4.0pt]
|u-y|-\varepsilon,&\text{ if }|u-y|>\varepsilon,\end{cases}

one has
M_{\ell}=\max\{0,\,2M-\varepsilon\}
and
L_{\ell}=1
.

Proof.

Squared loss.

\ell(u,y)=(u-y)^{2}
is maximized when
u
and
y
are at opposite ends of
[-M,M]
, giving
M_{\ell}=(2M)^{2}=4M^{2}
. Using that
\partial_{u}(u-y)^{2}=2(u-y)
, one gets that
|\partial_{u}(u-y)^{2}|=2|u-y|\leq 2(|u|+|y|)\leq 2(M+M)=4M=L_{\ell}
by the triangle inequality and using that
u,y\in[-M,M]
.

Absolute loss.
For
\ell(u,y)=|u-y|
we have
M_{\ell}=\sup_{u,y\in[-M,M]}|u-y|=2M
, since the residual
|u-y|
is maximized when
u
and
y
are at opposite ends of
[-M,M]
.
For
u\neq y
the partial derivative with respect to
u
equals
\partial_{u}|u-y|=\operatorname{sign}(u-y)
, and at
u=y
the subdifferential is
\partial_{u}|u-y|=[-1,1]
. Hence,
L_{\ell}=\sup_{u,y\in[-M,M]}|\partial_{u}\ell(u,y)|=1
.

Huber loss.
For parameter
\delta>0
, let

\displaystyle\ell(u,y;\delta)=\begin{cases}\tfrac{1}{2}(u-y)^{2},&\text{ if }|u-y|\leq\delta,\\[4.0pt]
\delta|u-y|-\tfrac{1}{2}\delta^{2},&\text{ if }|u-y|>\delta.\end{cases}

We discuss the
2
cases in the definition of
\ell
separately. We start with determining
L_{\ell}
.

•

Quadratic region (
|u-y|\leq\delta
): In this case, we have
\partial_{u}\ell(u,y;\delta)=u-y
, therefore
|\partial_{u}\ell(u,y;\delta)|\leq\delta
.

•

Linear region (
|u-y|>\delta
): In this case, we have
\partial_{u}\ell(u,y;\delta)=\delta\,\operatorname{sign}(u-y)
, hence
|\partial_{u}\ell(u,y;\delta)|=|\delta\,\operatorname{sign}(u-y)|=\delta
.

This means that
L_{\ell}=\sup_{u,y\in[-M,M]}|\partial_{u}\ell(u,y;\delta)|=\delta
.

We continue with computing
M_{\ell}
. The supremum of the loss occurs for
\sup_{u,y\in[-M,M]}|u-y|=2M
. If
2M\leq\delta
the quadratic branch applies and
M_{\ell}=\tfrac{1}{2}(2M)^{2}=2M^{2}
, while if
2M>\delta
the linear branch applies yielding
M_{\ell}=\delta(2M)-\tfrac{1}{2}\delta^{2}=2\delta M-\tfrac{1}{2}\delta^{2}
.

\varepsilon
-insensitive loss.
For
\varepsilon>0
, let

\displaystyle\ell(u,y;\varepsilon)=\begin{cases}0,&\text{ if }|u-y|\leq\varepsilon,\\[4.0pt]
|u-y|-\varepsilon,&\text{ if }|u-y|>\varepsilon.\end{cases}

The supremum of the loss occurs for
\sup_{u,y\in[-M,M]}|u-y|=2M
, hence
M_{\ell}=\max\{0,2M-\varepsilon\}
. Considering
L_{\ell}
, by using that

\displaystyle\partial_{u}\ell(u,y;\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\{-1\}&\text{ if }u<y-\varepsilon,\\
[-1,0]&\text{ if }u=y-\varepsilon,\\
\{0\}&\text{ if }u\in(y-\epsilon,y+\varepsilon),\\
[0,1]&\text{ if }u=y+\varepsilon,\\
\{1\}&\text{ if }u>y+\varepsilon,\end{cases}

one gets that
L_{\ell}=\sup_{u,y\in[-M,M]}|\partial_{u}\ell(u,y;\varepsilon)|=1
.
∎

Appendix D
External Lemmas

This section is dedicated to an external lemma used in our proofs.

Lemma 33

(Arzelà–Ascoli Theorem)
.

Let
(\mathcal{X},d)
be a compact metric space and let
\mathcal{F}\subseteq\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X})
. Then
\mathcal{F}
is relatively compact in
\left(\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X}),\|\cdot\|_{\infty}\right)
iff
\mathcal{F}
is equicontinuous and
\mathcal{F}
is bounded pointwise (in other words, for all
x\in\mathcal{X}
,
\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}|f(x)|<\infty
).
